{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01KVXxZtDDkF7ffh7vgB6cdC","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"NFR-PERF4 disk-budget poller (T03.19 / D-0060 / R-060).\n2\t\n3\tDesign-spec §4 (``--max-disk-mb``) and §13 (NFR-PERF4) require the\n4\torchestrator to bound the cumulative artifact size under\n5\t``--output-dir`` during a run. The motivation is the R4 risk row in\n6\tdesign-spec §10: a fan-out of 15 concurrent HOMEs with PTY transcripts\n7\tcan grow past several hundred megabytes on a slow eval; left unchecked,\n8\tthat fills the developer host (or a CI runner with a tight disk quota)\n9\tand corrupts every neighbouring run. The orchestrator addresses this\n10\tby polling the run-directory size every five seconds and signalling a\n11\t\"stop scheduling, drain in-flight, exit 2\" breach when the configured\n12\tbudget is exceeded.\n13\t\n14\tThis module implements the polling primitive. The integration with\n15\t:class:`RunOrchestrator` lives in ``orchestrator.py``; the CLI flag\n16\t(``--max-disk-mb``) is wired by the Phase 4 dispatcher per design-spec\n17\t§4. The poller is deliberately decoupled from both so it can be exercised\n18\tin isolation by the T03.19 fixture suite (``test_disk_budget.py``) and\n19\tre-used by any future component (eg. the doctor pre-flight) that needs\n20\ta cheap, side-effect-free disk gauge.\n21\t\n22\tPublic surface\n23\t==============\n24\t\n25\t:class:`DiskBudgetPoller`\n26\t    Background-thread poller. Constructed with an output directory and a\n27\t    ``max_disk_mb`` budget; ``start()`` / ``stop()`` (or the context-\n28\t    manager form) drive the background thread. Exposes :meth:`is_breached`\n29\t    so the orchestrator can short-circuit its submission loop and\n30\t    :meth:`breach_detail` for the partial-summary payload.\n31\t\n32\t:data:`DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_EXIT_CODE`\n33\t    Process exit code emitted by the Phase 4 dispatcher when the poller\n34\t    has flagged a breach. ``2`` per design-spec §4 (harness error).\n35\t\n36\t:data:`DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME`\n37\t    Filename of the side-car the poller writes inside the run directory\n38\t    on breach. The Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) surfaces this path under\n39\t    ``RunSummary.artifacts['disk_budget_exceeded']``; the filename is\n40\t    pinned here so consumers do not have to discover it by scan.\n41\t\n42\tBreach semantics\n43\t================\n44\t\n45\tWhen ``max_disk_mb > 0``:\n46\t\n47\t* The poller measures cumulative size of the output directory tree every\n48\t  ``tick_sec`` seconds (default ``5.0``). The first tick fires *after*\n49\t  the initial sleep so an empty run directory does not get flagged on\n50\t  start-up.\n51\t* When measured usage exceeds ``max_disk_mb * 1024 * 1024`` bytes, the\n52\t  poller writes ``DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME`` into the output\n53\t  directory, sets its internal ``is_breached()`` flag, and exits the\n54\t  loop. The flag is one-shot — once set, the poller stays in the\n55\t  breached state for the rest of its lifetime.\n56\t* The orchestrator polls ``is_breached()`` between submissions; on a\n57\t  breach it stops scheduling new specs and synthesises a\n58\t  ``SKIPPED`` outcome (skip_reason ``\"disk_budget_exceeded\"``) for every\n59\t  unsubmitted spec. In-flight workers continue to completion — the\n60\t  poller never kills a thread or sends a signal.\n61\t\n62\tWhen ``max_disk_mb == 0``:\n63\t\n64\t* The poller is *disabled*. ``start()`` is a no-op, the background\n65\t  thread is never spawned, and :meth:`is_breached` always returns\n66\t  ``False``. The orchestrator behaves as if no poller were wired.\n67\t  Design-spec §4 documents ``--max-disk-mb 0`` as the \"disable\"\n68\t  invocation.\n69\t\n70\tThread safety\n71\t=============\n72\t\n73\tThe breach state is stored as a :class:`threading.Event`. Reading the\n74\tdetail mapping after :meth:`is_breached` returns ``True`` is safe because\n75\tthe detail is published *before* the event is set (release-then-set\n76\tordering). The poller thread is the only writer of the detail; the\n77\torchestrator (main) thread is the only reader. Both ends communicate\n78\tthrough the event without explicit locking.\n79\t\"\"\"\n80\t\n81\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n82\t\n83\timport json\n84\timport threading\n85\timport time\n86\tfrom dataclasses import dataclass\n87\tfrom pathlib import Path\n88\tfrom typing import Mapping, Optional\n89\t\n90\t__all__ = [\n91\t    \"DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME\",\n92\t    \"DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_EXIT_CODE\",\n93\t    \"DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_REASON\",\n94\t    \"DEFAULT_DISK_BUDGET_MB\",\n95\t    \"DEFAULT_DISK_POLL_TICK_SEC\",\n96\t    \"DiskBudgetPoller\",\n97\t]\n98\t\n99\t\n100\t# Process exit code returned by the Phase 4 CLI dispatcher when the\n101\t# poller has signalled a breach. Matches the design-spec §4 exit-code\n102\t# table: ``2`` = harness error. The disk-budget breach is a harness-\n103\t# enforced abort, not a per-eval failure, so it uses the same code as\n104\t# the loader / scratch-root / reporter-contract violations.\n105\tDISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_EXIT_CODE: int = 2\n106\t\n107\t# Filename the poller writes when a breach is detected. Pinned here so\n108\t# the orchestrator, Reporter, and integration tests all reference one\n109\t# constant. ``.json`` because the payload is a structured\n110\t# :class:`BreachDetail` projection.\n111\tDISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME: str = \"disk_budget_exceeded.json\"\n112\t\n113\t# ``skip_reason`` token written into every synthesised SKIPPED outcome\n114\t# the orchestrator emits for unsubmitted specs after a breach. The motivation is the R4 risk row in\ndesign-spec §10: a fan-out of 15 concurrent HOMEs with PTY transcripts\ncan grow past several hundred megabytes on a slow eval; left unchecked,\nthat fills the developer host (or a CI runner with a tight disk quota)\nand corrupts every neighbouring run. The orchestrator addresses this\nby polling the run-directory size every five seconds and signalling a\n\"stop scheduling, drain in-flight, exit 2\" breach when the configured\nbudget is exceeded.\n\nThis module implements the polling primitive. The integration with\n:class:`RunOrchestrator` lives in ``orchestrator.py``; the CLI flag\n(``--max-disk-mb``) is wired by the Phase 4 dispatcher per design-spec\n§4. The poller is deliberately decoupled from both so it can be exercised\nin isolation by the T03.19 fixture suite (``test_disk_budget.py``) and\nre-used by any future component (eg. the doctor pre-flight) that needs\na cheap, side-effect-free disk gauge.\n\nPublic surface\n==============\n\n:class:`DiskBudgetPoller`\n    Background-thread poller. Constructed with an output directory and a\n    ``max_disk_mb`` budget; ``start()`` / ``stop()`` (or the context-\n    manager form) drive the background thread. Exposes :meth:`is_breached`\n    so the orchestrator can short-circuit its submission loop and\n    :meth:`breach_detail` for the partial-summary payload.\n\n:data:`DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_EXIT_CODE`\n    Process exit code emitted by the Phase 4 dispatcher when the poller\n    has flagged a breach. ``2`` per design-spec §4 (harness error).\n\n:data:`DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME`\n    Filename of the side-car the poller writes inside the run directory\n    on breach. The Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) surfaces this path under\n    ``RunSummary.artifacts['disk_budget_exceeded']``; the filename is\n    pinned here so consumers do not have to discover it by scan.\n\nBreach semantics\n================\n\nWhen ``max_disk_mb > 0``:\n\n* The poller measures cumulative size of the output directory tree every\n  ``tick_sec`` seconds (default ``5.0``). The first tick fires *after*\n  the initial sleep so an empty run directory does not get flagged on\n  start-up.\n* When measured usage exceeds ``max_disk_mb * 1024 * 1024`` bytes, the\n  poller writes ``DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME`` into the output\n  directory, sets its internal ``is_breached()`` flag, and exits the\n  loop. The flag is one-shot — once set, the poller stays in the\n  breached state for the rest of its lifetime.\n* The orchestrator polls ``is_breached()`` between submissions; on a\n  breach it stops scheduling new specs and synthesises a\n  ``SKIPPED`` outcome (skip_reason ``\"disk_budget_exceeded\"``) for every\n  unsubmitted spec. In-flight workers continue to completion — the\n  poller never kills a thread or sends a signal.\n\nWhen ``max_disk_mb == 0``:\n\n* The poller is *disabled*. ``start()`` is a no-op, the background\n  thread is never spawned, and :meth:`is_breached` always returns\n  ``False``. The orchestrator behaves as if no poller were wired.\n  Design-spec §4 documents ``--max-disk-mb 0`` as the \"disable\"\n  invocation.\n\nThread safety\n=============\n\nThe breach state is stored as a :class:`threading.Event`. Reading the\ndetail mapping after :meth:`is_breached` returns ``True`` is safe because\nthe detail is published *before* the event is set (release-then-set\nordering). The poller thread is the only writer of the detail; the\norchestrator (main) thread is the only reader. Both ends communicate\nthrough the event without explicit locking.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nimport threading\nimport time\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Mapping, Optional\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME\",\n    \"DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_EXIT_CODE\",\n    \"DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_REASON\",\n    \"DEFAULT_DISK_BUDGET_MB\",\n    \"DEFAULT_DISK_POLL_TICK_SEC\",\n    \"DiskBudgetPoller\",\n]\n\n\n# Process exit code returned by the Phase 4 CLI dispatcher when the\n# poller has signalled a breach. Matches the design-spec §4 exit-code\n# table: ``2`` = harness error. The disk-budget breach is a harness-\n# enforced abort, not a per-eval failure, so it uses the same code as\n# the loader / scratch-root / reporter-contract violations.\nDISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_EXIT_CODE: int = 2\n\n# Filename the poller writes when a breach is detected. Pinned here so\n# the orchestrator, Reporter, and integration tests all reference one\n# constant. ``.json`` because the payload is a structured\n# :class:`BreachDetail` projection.\nDISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME: str = \"disk_budget_exceeded.json\"\n\n# ``skip_reason`` token written into every synthesised SKIPPED outcome\n# the orchestrator emits for unsubmitted specs after a breach. Tests substitute a\n        unique name when several pollers share a directory.\n    clock:\n        Optional ``() -> str`` returning the ``ticked_at`` ISO-8601\n        timestamp. Tests inject a deterministic clock; the default\n        formatter uses :func:`time.gmtime` so production output is\n        timezone-stable.\n\n    Notes\n    -----\n    The recursive size walk uses :meth:`pathlib.Path.rglob` and\n    :meth:`pathlib.Path.stat` with ``follow_symlinks=False`` so the\n    poller cannot be tricked into double-counting via a symlink into a\n    sibling tree. Files that disappear between the directory walk and\n    the ``stat()`` call (eg. an in-flight runner cleaning up its scratch\n    HOME) are silently ignored — a transient OS error must not crash the\n    poller thread.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    DEFAULT_TICK_SEC = DEFAULT_DISK_POLL_TICK_SEC\n    DEFAULT_MAX_DISK_MB = DEFAULT_DISK_BUDGET_MB\n    ARTIFACT_NAME = DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME\n    BREACH_REASON = DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_REASON\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        output_dir: Path,\n        *,\n        max_disk_mb: int = DEFAULT_DISK_BUDGET_MB,\n        tick_sec: float = DEFAULT_DISK_POLL_TICK_SEC,\n        artifact_name: str = DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME,\n        ensure_output_dir: bool = False,\n        clock: Optional[\"callable[[], str]\"] = None,\n    ) -> None:\n        if not isinstance(max_disk_mb, int) or isinstance(max_disk_mb, bool):\n            raise TypeError(\n                f\"max_disk_mb must be int; got {type(max_disk_mb).__name__}\"\n            )\n        if max_disk_mb < 0:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"max_disk_mb must be >= 0; got {max_disk_mb} \"\n                \"(use 0 to disable the budget)\"\n            )\n        if tick_sec <= 0:\n            raise ValueError(f\"tick_sec must be > 0; got {tick_sec}\")\n        if not artifact_name:\n            raise ValueError(\"artifact_name must be non-empty\")\n\n        self._output_dir = Path(output_dir)\n        if ensure_output_dir:\n            self._output_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n\n        self._max_disk_mb = int(max_disk_mb)\n        self._budget_bytes = int(max_disk_mb) * _BYTES_PER_MB\n        self._tick_sec = float(tick_sec)\n        self._artifact_name = artifact_name\n        self._clock = clock or _default_iso_clock\n\n        self._stop_event = threading.Event()\n        self._breach_event = threading.Event()\n        self._detail: Optional[BreachDetail] = None\n        self._artifact_path: Optional[Path] = None\n        self._thread: Optional[threading.Thread] = None\n        self._lifecycle_lock = threading.Lock()\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Read-only inspection surface\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    @property\n    def enabled(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"``True`` when the poller will actually spawn a thread.\"\"\"\n        return self._max_disk_mb > 0\n\n    @property\n    def max_disk_mb(self) -> int:\n        return self._max_disk_mb\n\n    @property\n    def budget_bytes(self) -> int:\n        return self._budget_bytes\n\n    @property\n    def tick_sec(self) -> float:\n        return self._tick_sec\n\n    @property\n    def output_dir(self) -> Path:\n        return self._output_dir\n\n    @property\n    def artifact_name(self) -> str:\n        return self._artifact_name\n\n    def is_breached(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"``True`` once a breach has been recorded.\n\n        One-shot — once the flag flips it never resets for the lifetime\n        of the poller. The orchestrator polls this between submissions\n        to decide whether to keep scheduling.\n        \"\"\"\n        return self._breach_event.is_set()\n\n    def breach_detail(self) -> Optional[Mapping[str, object]]:\n        \"\"\"Return the breach payload as a plain dict, or ``None``.\n\n        The mapping mirrors :class:`BreachDetail.to_dict`. Returns\n        ``None`` until :meth:`is_breached` reports ``True``.\n        \"\"\"\n        detail = self._detail\n        if detail is None:\n            return None\n        return detail.to_dict()\n\n    def artifact_path(self) -> Optional[Path]:\n        \"\"\"Path of the side-car file the poller wrote, or ``None``.\n\n        Equal to ``output_dir / artifact_name`` once a breach has been\n        recorded; ``None`` while the poller is healthy.\n        \"\"\"\n        return self._artifact_path\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Lifecycle\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def start(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Spawn the background polling thread.\n\n        No-op when :attr:`enabled` is ``False`` (``max_disk_mb=0``) or\n        when the thread is already running. Idempotent so the\n        orchestrator can call :meth:`start` unconditionally without\n        guarding the disabled case.\n        \"\"\"\n        with self._lifecycle_lock:\n            if not self.enabled:\n                return\n            if self._thread is not None and self._thread.is_alive():\n                return\n            self._stop_event.clear()\n            self._thread = threading.Thread(\n                target=self._poll_loop,\n                name=f\"cliEval-disk-budget-{self._max_disk_mb}MB\",\n                daemon=True,\n            )\n            self._thread.start()\n\n    def stop(self, *, timeout: Optional[float] = None) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Signal the background thread to exit and join it.\n\n        ``timeout`` is forwarded to :meth:`threading.Thread.join`. The\n        method is idempotent and safe to call from any thread; signal-\n        handler installers MAY call it from the main thread without\n        knowing whether the poller was ever started.\n        \"\"\"\n        with self._lifecycle_lock:\n            thread = self._thread\n            self._stop_event.set()\n        if thread is not None:\n            thread.join(timeout=timeout)\n\n    def __enter__(self) -> \"DiskBudgetPoller\":\n        self.start()\n        return self\n\n    def __exit__(self, exc_type, exc, tb) -> None:\n        self.stop()\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Polling internals\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _poll_loop(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Tick-driven measurement loop.\n\n        Uses :meth:`threading.Event.wait` rather than :func:`time.sleep`\n        so :meth:`stop` can shortcut the next tick immediately — the\n        orchestrator does not want to wait up to ``tick_sec`` seconds\n        for the poller to notice it is being shut down.\n        \"\"\"\n        while not self._stop_event.wait(self._tick_sec):\n            usage_bytes = self._compute_usage()\n            if usage_bytes > self._budget_bytes:\n                self._record_breach(usage_bytes)\n                return\n\n    def _compute_usage(self) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Sum every regular file under ``output_dir`` non-recursively-of-symlinks.\n\n        Returns ``0`` when the directory does not exist yet; lets the\n        poller run before the orchestrator has created its run\n        directory without raising. Per-file ``OSError`` is swallowed so\n        a vanishing in-flight scratch file does not crash the poller.\n        \"\"\"\n        root = self._output_dir\n        if not root.is_dir():\n            return 0\n        total = 0\n        # ``rglob('*')`` returns every entry including directories and\n        # symlinks. We only count regular files (``is_file()`` returns\n        # ``False`` for symlinks to files thanks to ``follow_symlinks``\n        # behaviour of the underlying ``stat`` — but we also guard with\n        # ``is_symlink()`` to be explicit about the policy).\n        for entry in root.rglob(\"*\"):\n            if entry.is_symlink():\n                continue\n            try:\n                if not entry.is_file():\n                    continue\n                total += entry.stat().st_size\n            except (OSError, FileNotFoundError):\n                # Transient: file was reaped between ``rglob`` and\n                # ``stat``. Skip — the next tick will catch any\n                # persistent growth.\n                continue\n        return total\n\n    def _record_breach(self, usage_bytes: int) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Persist the side-car and flip the breach flag.\n\n        The detail is built *before* the side-car is written so a\n        partial-write failure (eg. directory was deleted out from under\n        us) still leaves a valid in-memory payload for the Reporter.\n        The breach flag is set *after* the detail is published so any\n        consumer that reads :meth:`breach_detail` after observing\n        :meth:`is_breached` ``True`` is guaranteed a non-``None``\n        return.\n        \"\"\"\n        detail = BreachDetail(\n            reason=self.BREACH_REASON,\n            output_dir=str(self._output_dir),\n            usage_bytes=int(usage_bytes),\n            budget_bytes=self._budget_bytes,\n            max_disk_mb=self._max_disk_mb,\n            ticked_at=self._clock(),\n        )\n        artifact_path = self._output_dir / self._artifact_name\n        try:\n            self._output_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n            artifact_path.write_text(\n                json.dumps(detail.to_dict(), indent=2, sort_keys=True) + \"\\n\",\n                encoding=\"utf-8\",\n            )\n        except OSError:\n            # Filesystem unavailable — record the breach in memory only\n            # so the orchestrator still observes the flag. The side-car\n            # absence is a known degraded mode documented in the spec.\n            artifact_path = None\n\n        # Publish detail before flipping the flag (release-then-set\n        # ordering — see module docstring).\n        self._detail = detail\n        self._artifact_path = artifact_path\n        self._breach_event.set()\n\n\ndef _default_iso_clock() -> str:\n    \"\"\"Return the current UTC time as an ISO-8601 ``Z`` string.\n\n    Centralised so :class:`DiskBudgetPoller` and the side-car payload\n    use a single timestamp format. Tests inject a deterministic clock\n    via the constructor so we never have to mock ``time``.\n    \"\"\"\n    return time.strftime(\"%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ\", time.gmtime())\n","numLines":464,"startLine":1,"totalLines":464}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01AgE81nLSXVtmeNfrsdWKHT","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"COMP-003 RunOrchestrator — parallel eval scheduler.\n2\t\n3\tTask T03.15 / Deliverable D-0057 / Roadmap row R-057 (COMP-003).\n4\t\n5\tThe orchestrator is the single component that turns a sequence of\n6\texpanded :class:`EvalSpec` rows into a sequence of :class:`EvalOutcome`\n7\trows by scheduling per-spec :class:`EvalRunner` workers through\n8\t``concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor + as_completed`` — the AC6\n9\tpattern the design-spec pins for cliEval. The implementation deliberately\n10\tmirrors the shape used by ``src/superclaude/cli/prd/executor.py:774-802``\n11\tso a reader switching between the PRD pipeline and the eval harness sees\n12\tthe same scheduling skeleton.\n13\t\n14\tResponsibilities\n15\t================\n16\t\n17\tThe orchestrator owns three cross-cutting concerns the lifecycle layer\n18\tintentionally defers:\n19\t\n20\t1. **Concurrency budget.** ``max_workers`` is clamped to ``[1, 15]`` per\n21\t   design-spec §11 (NFR-PERF2) with a default of ``8``. Values outside\n22\t   the range either raise (``parallel < 1``) or saturate to the upper\n23\t   bound (``parallel > 15``). The clamp is enforced once at the top of\n24\t   :meth:`RunOrchestrator.run` so the rest of the body can trust the\n25\t   effective value.\n26\t2. **Outcome contract.** :class:`RunOrchestrator` guarantees one\n27\t   :class:`EvalOutcome` per input :class:`EvalSpec` in the original\n28\t   sequence order. The Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) enforces the\n29\t   N'-vs-K invariant on top of this — the orchestrator never elides a\n30\t   spec.\n31\t3. **Cooperative cancellation.** When a shared\n32\t   :class:`CancellationToken` is wired (NFR-REL1 / T03.07), the\n33\t   orchestrator stops submitting new work as soon as it observes the\n34\t   token cancelled. Specs that were never submitted synthesise an\n35\t   ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome at the orchestrator layer so the per-spec\n36\t   contract still holds.  In-flight runners observe the same token via\n37\t   :class:`EvalRunner` and convert worker-thread\n38\t   ``KeyboardInterrupt`` / ``SystemExit`` into ``INTERRUPTED`` outcomes\n39\t   themselves — the orchestrator never has to translate an exception\n40\t   into a status.\n41\t\n42\tThe orchestrator deliberately does *not* install signal handlers. Signal\n43\tinstallation is the CLI dispatcher's job (Phase 4): the dispatcher\n44\tallocates a :class:`CancellationToken`, wraps the orchestrator call in a\n45\t:class:`SignalHandlerInstaller`, and hands the token down so the eval\n46\trunners observe the same cancellation flag the signal handler flips.\n47\t\n48\tWorker contract\n49\t===============\n50\t\n51\tThe orchestrator does not construct :class:`EvalRunner` instances\n52\titself. Building an :class:`EvalRunner` requires per-eval HOME\n53\tisolation, scratch paths, executor wiring, and an expect-callable\n54\tsequence — none of which the orchestrator can resolve. Instead the\n55\tcaller supplies a ``run_one: Callable[[EvalSpec], EvalOutcome]`` worker\n56\tthat wraps everything from ``HomeIsolation`` allocation through\n57\t:meth:`EvalRunner.run` into a single closure. Production callers will\n58\ttypically write::\n59\t\n60\t    def run_one(spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n61\t        home = HomeIsolation(eval_id=spec.id, ...)\n62\t        runner = EvalRunner(home=home, ...)\n63\t        return runner.run(spec)\n64\t\n65\tThis indirection keeps the orchestrator a pure scheduling primitive and\n66\tmakes unit-testing trivial — tests substitute a stub worker that returns\n67\ta canned outcome without touching the filesystem.\n68\t\n69\tIf the worker raises (e.g. construction failure, unexpected propagation\n70\tof ``KeyboardInterrupt`` because the caller declined to wire a token),\n71\tthe orchestrator folds the exception into an ``ERRORED`` outcome\n72\trecording the fully-qualified exception classname — the same shape\n73\t:func:`run_eval` (T03.04) emits when a harness step fails. This keeps\n74\tthe per-spec contract intact even when a worker misbehaves.\n75\t\"\"\"\n76\t\n77\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n78\t\n79\tfrom concurrent.futures import Future, ThreadPoolExecutor, as_completed\n80\tfrom typing import Callable, ClassVar, Optional, Sequence\n81\t\n82\tfrom .disk_budget import DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_REASON, DiskBudgetPoller\n83\tfrom .models import EvalOutcome, EvalSpec\n84\tfrom .signal_handler import CancellationToken\n85\t\n86\t__all__ = [\"RunOrchestrator\", \"EvalWorker\"]\n87\t\n88\t\n89\t# A worker callable receives an :class:`EvalSpec` and returns the\n90\t# corresponding :class:`EvalOutcome`. The implementation deliberately\nmirrors the shape used by ``src/superclaude/cli/prd/executor.py:774-802``\nso a reader switching between the PRD pipeline and the eval harness sees\nthe same scheduling skeleton.\n\nResponsibilities\n================\n\nThe orchestrator owns three cross-cutting concerns the lifecycle layer\nintentionally defers:\n\n1. **Concurrency budget.** ``max_workers`` is clamped to ``[1, 15]`` per\n   design-spec §11 (NFR-PERF2) with a default of ``8``. Values outside\n   the range either raise (``parallel < 1``) or saturate to the upper\n   bound (``parallel > 15``). The clamp is enforced once at the top of\n   :meth:`RunOrchestrator.run` so the rest of the body can trust the\n   effective value.\n2. **Outcome contract.** :class:`RunOrchestrator` guarantees one\n   :class:`EvalOutcome` per input :class:`EvalSpec` in the original\n   sequence order. The Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) enforces the\n   N'-vs-K invariant on top of this — the orchestrator never elides a\n   spec.\n3. **Cooperative cancellation.** When a shared\n   :class:`CancellationToken` is wired (NFR-REL1 / T03.07), the\n   orchestrator stops submitting new work as soon as it observes the\n   token cancelled. Specs that were never submitted synthesise an\n   ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome at the orchestrator layer so the per-spec\n   contract still holds.  In-flight runners observe the same token via\n   :class:`EvalRunner` and convert worker-thread\n   ``KeyboardInterrupt`` / ``SystemExit`` into ``INTERRUPTED`` outcomes\n   themselves — the orchestrator never has to translate an exception\n   into a status.\n\nThe orchestrator deliberately does *not* install signal handlers. Signal\ninstallation is the CLI dispatcher's job (Phase 4): the dispatcher\nallocates a :class:`CancellationToken`, wraps the orchestrator call in a\n:class:`SignalHandlerInstaller`, and hands the token down so the eval\nrunners observe the same cancellation flag the signal handler flips.\n\nWorker contract\n===============\n\nThe orchestrator does not construct :class:`EvalRunner` instances\nitself. Building an :class:`EvalRunner` requires per-eval HOME\nisolation, scratch paths, executor wiring, and an expect-callable\nsequence — none of which the orchestrator can resolve. Instead the\ncaller supplies a ``run_one: Callable[[EvalSpec], EvalOutcome]`` worker\nthat wraps everything from ``HomeIsolation`` allocation through\n:meth:`EvalRunner.run` into a single closure. Production callers will\ntypically write::\n\n    def run_one(spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n        home = HomeIsolation(eval_id=spec.id, ...)\n        runner = EvalRunner(home=home, ...)\n        return runner.run(spec)\n\nThis indirection keeps the orchestrator a pure scheduling primitive and\nmakes unit-testing trivial — tests substitute a stub worker that returns\na canned outcome without touching the filesystem.\n\nIf the worker raises (e.g. construction failure, unexpected propagation\nof ``KeyboardInterrupt`` because the caller declined to wire a token),\nthe orchestrator folds the exception into an ``ERRORED`` outcome\nrecording the fully-qualified exception classname — the same shape\n:func:`run_eval` (T03.04) emits when a harness step fails. Returns the\n        :class:`EvalOutcome` for that spec. Worker exceptions are folded\n        into an ``ERRORED`` outcome — the orchestrator never re-raises\n        out of :meth:`run`.\n    cancellation_token:\n        Optional :class:`CancellationToken` shared with the CLI\n        dispatcher's signal handler (NFR-REL1 / T03.07). They\n        # receive a synthesised ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome below.\n        cancelled_indices: list[int] = []\n        # Indices for specs that were never submitted because the\n        # disk-budget poller flipped its breach flag. Already-running workers observe\n                    # the token themselves (via :class:`EvalRunner`) and\n                    # convert to INTERRUPTED outcomes; we synthesise an\n                    # INTERRUPTED row for every spec that did not even\n                    # reach the pool so the per-spec contract still holds.\n                    #\n                    # Cancellation is checked *before* the disk-budget\n                    # breach so a SIGINT received concurrently with a\n                    # breach routes the spec to INTERRUPTED (operator\n                    # intent dominates a resource limit).\n                    if self._is_cancelled():\n                        cancelled_indices.append(index)\n                        continue\n                    if self._is_disk_budget_exceeded():\n                        disk_budget_skipped_indices.append(index)\n                        continue\n                    future = pool.submit(self._invoke_worker, spec)\n                    futures[future] = index\n\n                # Drain the pool. ``as_completed`` is the AC6 idiom — it\n                # yields each future the instant its worker returns,\n                # letting downstream readers (telemetry, TUI) update in\n                # near real-time without waiting for the slowest eval.\n                # In-flight workers continue to completion even after a\n                # disk-budget breach — the AC6 contract requires \"in-\n                # flight evals complete\" before the run exits.\n                for future in as_completed(futures):\n                    index = futures[future]\n                    spec = specs[index]\n                    try:\n                        outcomes[index] = future.result()\n                    except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — see docstring\n                        # The worker should fold its own harness errors\n                        # into an ERRORED outcome via :func:`run_eval`. We\n                        # only get here when the worker itself raised\n                        # (construction failure, unwired-token\n                        # KeyboardInterrupt propagation, etc.). Translate\n                        # to ERRORED so :meth:`run` never re-raises.\n                        outcomes[index] = self._errored_outcome(spec, exc)\n        finally:\n            if poller is not None:\n                poller.stop()\n\n        # Backfill INTERRUPTED outcomes for never-submitted specs after\n        # the pool has shut down so we cannot race with an in-flight\n        # cancellation.\n        for index in cancelled_indices:\n            outcomes[index] = self._interrupted_outcome(specs[index])\n        # Backfill SKIPPED outcomes for specs we declined to submit\n        # because the disk budget was breached. Same post-shutdown\n        # placement as the INTERRUPTED backfill — once we are here the\n        # pool has drained, so the synthesised outcomes cannot race\n        # with an in-flight result.\n        for index in disk_budget_skipped_indices:\n            outcomes[index] = self._disk_budget_skipped_outcome(specs[index])\n\n        # Every slot must be populated by now. Mirrors :meth:`_is_cancelled` so the submission\n        loop reads naturally.\n        \"\"\"\n        poller = self._disk_budget_poller\n        return poller is not None and {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_017qXRtP7xkkG6cQdYyPeL8Z","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# D-0080 — TEST-009 artifact reproducibility tests\n2\t\n3\t**Roadmap row:** R-080 (TEST-009)\n4\t**Phase task:** T04.20 (phase-4-tasklist.md §T04.20)\n5\t**Test module:** `tests/cli/eval/test_artifact_reproducibility.py`\n6\t**Producers under test:**\n7\t- `src/superclaude/cli/eval/artifact_layout.py` (FR-G4 / R-074 / D-0074 / T04.13)\n8\t  - `compose_run_dir`, `compose_run_id`, `compose_per_eval_dir`,\n9\t    `allocate_per_eval_paths`, `parse_run_dir_components`\n10\t  - Constants: `RUN_DIR_PREFIX` (`.dev/eval-runs`), `PER_EVAL_DIRNAME`\n11\t    (`per-eval`), `LOGS_JSONL_NAME` (`logs.jsonl`),\n12\t    `TTY_TRANSCRIPT_NAME` (`tty.transcript`), `ARTIFACTS_SUBDIR_NAME`\n13\t    (`artifacts`)\n14\t- `src/superclaude/cli/eval/models.py::EvalOutcome.artifacts` (DM-001)\n15\t- `src/superclaude/cli/eval/models.py::ExpectFailure.traceback` (DM-005)\n16\t- `src/superclaude/cli/eval/reporter.py::Reporter.write` (DM-012,\n17\t  COMP-008, FR-RPT1)\n18\t\n19\t## 1. Goal\n20\t\n21\tPin the **reproducible artifact layout** every `superclaude eval run`\n22\tinvocation must produce so that:\n23\t\n24\t1. Two operators (or CI shards) replaying the same suite at the same\n25\t   wall-clock instant land on **byte-identical** trees — no drift in\n26\t   path shape, no drift in the per-eval emitter filenames, no drift in\n27\t   the summary.json cross-link strings.\n28\t2. An ERRORED eval **always** carries a Python traceback through both\n29\t   the JSONL event log (live, append-only) and the rendered\n30\t   `summary.json` (post-run, archived). A future refactor that loses\n31\t   the traceback at either layer fails loudly here.\n32\t3. The `summary.json` `evals[]` entries name per-eval artifact paths\n33\t   relative to the run directory — so a reviewer can `cat` a\n34\t   transcript from the summary without re-deriving the FR-G4 layout\n35\t   formula.\n36\t\n37\tThe full end-to-end `superclaude eval run` orchestrator loop is a\n38\tT04.10 forward dependency. This module deliberately anchors the\n39\tcontract at the **layout + Reporter seam**: it builds the FR-G4 tree\n40\tvia the `artifact_layout` helpers directly, materializes per-eval\n41\tfiles, then drives `Reporter.write()`. The T04.10 closure must satisfy\n42\tthe same invariants when it lands; nothing in this test couples to a\n43\tspecific run-loop wiring.\n44\t\n45\t## 2. Reproducibility matrix\n46\t\n47\tThe eight pytest cases in `tests/cli/eval/test_artifact_reproducibility.py`\n48\tcover the five acceptance criteria from phase-4-tasklist.md §T04.20\n49\tplus three reinforcing guards. Cross-reference:\n50\t\n51\t| # | Test function | AC covered | Pins |\n52\t|---|---|---|---|\n53\t| 1 | `test_run_dir_matches_fr_g4_pattern` | AC-1: run dir matches `.dev/eval-runs/<ISO>/<run-id>/` | Regex pin on `<output_root>/.dev/eval-runs/YYYY-MM-DD/HHMMSSZ-<8-hex>/`. `parse_run_dir_components` round-trips `(date, run_id)` from the path. |\n54\t| 2 | `test_run_dir_deterministic_for_inputs` | AC-1 + reproducibility invariant | Same `(suite_name, started_at)` → byte-identical run dir. Different `suite_name` OR different `started_at` → different run-id (sha256-deterministic per T04.13). |\n55\t| 3 | `test_per_eval_logs_jsonl_present_and_parsable` | AC-2: `logs.jsonl` exists per eval | The JSONL lives at the pinned filename inside `per-eval/<eval_id>/`, every line is JSON-parseable, the file is non-empty. |\n56\t| 4 | `test_per_eval_tty_transcript_present_and_non_empty` | AC-3: `tty.transcript` exists per eval | `tty.transcript` lands at the pinned filename inside `per-eval/<eval_id>/`, non-empty bytes. |\n57\t| 5 | `test_errored_outcome_records_stack_trace` | AC-4: traceback on ERRORED | A real `RuntimeError` is raised + captured; the resulting traceback appears VERBATIM in both (a) the JSONL `result.failure` event and (b) the rendered `summary.json` `evals[].expects[0].failure.traceback`. |\n58\t| 6 | `test_summary_json_cross_links_per_eval_artifacts` | AC-5: `evals[].artifacts` cross-link | Every `evals[]` row's `artifacts` mapping names `logs.jsonl`, `tty.transcript`, and the `artifacts/` directory; each cited POSIX path resolves to a real file/dir under the run directory. |\n59\t| 7 | `test_artifact_tree_reproducible_across_replays` | AC-1 + AC-5 reinforcing | Two independent replays of the same `(suite_name, started_at, eval set)` produce **byte-identical** `summary.json` outputs (modulo the run-dir prefix), proving the cross-link strings are stable across replays. |\n60\t| 8 | `test_parse_run_dir_rejects_non_layout_paths` | Negative guard | `parse_run_dir_components` raises on paths that do not match the FR-G4 layout — guards against silent acceptance of malformed paths. |\n61\t\n62\t## 3. FR-G4 layout pinned by this module\n63\t\n64\t```\n65\t<output_root>/\n66\t└── .dev/eval-runs/                           # RUN_DIR_PREFIX\n67\t    └── <YYYY-MM-DD>/                         # date component of started_at\n68\t        └── <HHMMSSZ>-<8-hex>/                # run_id = compose_run_id(suite_name, started_at)\n69\t            ├── summary.json                  # Reporter.write target (DM-012)\n70\t            └── per-eval/                     # PER_EVAL_DIRNAME\n71\t                └── <eval_id>/                # one subtree per EvalOutcome\n72\t                    ├── logs.jsonl            # LOGS_JSONL_NAME\n73\t                    ├── tty.transcript        # TTY_TRANSCRIPT_NAME\n74\t                    └── artifacts/            # ARTIFACTS_SUBDIR_NAME\n75\t```\n76\t\n77\tThe `<8-hex>` suffix of the run-id is `sha256(suite_name || ISO-Z-instant)[:8]` —\n78\tdeterministic, so two replays collide on the same path. This is the\n79\tload-bearing reproducibility guarantee tests 1, 2, and 7 exercise.\n80\t\n81\t## 4. Cross-link contract (AC-5 detail)\n82\t\n83\tPer DM-001, each `EvalOutcome.artifacts` is a `Mapping[str, str]`\n84\twhere:\n85\t- keys are the well-known names `\"logs.jsonl\"`, `\"tty.transcript\"`,\n86\t  `\"artifacts\"`;\n87\t- values are POSIX paths **relative to the run directory** (not\n88\t  absolute, not relative to CWD).\n89\t\n90\tTest 6 verifies the cross-link by resolving every value against the\n91\trun directory and asserting the resulting path exists. The\n92\t\"relative-to-run-dir\" convention is what makes the artifact tree\n93\t**relocatable**: a CI job can tar the run dir and a reviewer can\n94\textract it anywhere on disk, and every cross-link in `summary.json`\n95\tstill resolves.\n96\t\n97\t## 5. Stack-trace channel duality (AC-4 detail)\n98\t\n99\tA real Python traceback is generated by raising and catching a\n100\t`RuntimeError` inside the test fixture (rather than hand-crafting a\n101\tmulti-line string), so the test exercises the exact stringification\n102\tcontract production code will hit: `traceback.format_exception(...)`\n103\toutput, line breaks preserved, indentation preserved.\n104\t\n105\tThe test then asserts the **same traceback string** appears in two\n106\tplaces:\n107\t\n108\t1. **JSONL channel** (`logs.jsonl`, the live event log) — the failure\n109\t   event carries `result.failure.traceback`. This is the\n110\t   append-only stream T03.05 / D-0049 emits during the run.\n111\t2. **Summary channel** (`summary.json` `evals[].expects[].failure`)\n112\t   — `ExpectFailure.traceback` (DM-005) carries the same string,\n113\t   serialized by `Reporter.to_json()` post-run.\n114\t\n115\tHolding both pins in one test guards against a future refactor that\n116\tdrops the field at either layer (e.g., truncating the traceback in the\n117\tsummary while keeping it in the JSONL, or vice versa).\n118\t\n119\t## 6. T04.10 forward dependency\n120\t\n121\tThe full `superclaude eval run` loop has not landed (it is T04.10);\n122\tthis module therefore exercises the layout + Reporter contract via\n123\tdirect calls to `compose_run_dir`, `allocate_per_eval_paths`, and\n124\t`Reporter.write()`, materializing per-eval files inside `tmp_path`.\n125\t\n126\tNo tests in this module are skipped — every guard runs today. When\n127\tT04.10 lands, the orchestrator must produce a tree that satisfies the\n128\tsame invariants this module pins. The handoff is asymmetric: T04.10\n129\twill reuse the same `artifact_layout` helpers (FR-G4 / D-0074), so a\n130\tT04.10 closure that emits these helpers is automatically conformant.\n131\t\n132\t## 7. Cross-links\n145\t\n146\t* FR-G4 / R-074 / D-0074 / T04.13 (`artifact_layout.py`) — the layout\n147\t  source-of-truth this module exercises directly. T04.13's\n148\t  `test_artifact_layout.py` pins the helper contract in isolation; this\n149\t  module pins the **end-to-end** layout + cross-link + traceback\n150\t  contract on top of those helpers.\n151\t* DM-001 / T01.12 (`EvalOutcome.artifacts`) — the cross-link field\n152\t  test 6 reads.\n153\t* DM-005 / T01.16 (`ExpectFailure.traceback`) — the traceback channel\n154\t  test 5 reads.\n155\t* DM-012 / T03.10 (`summary.schema.json`) — the schema the Reporter\n156\t  renders to; Goal\n\nPin the **reproducible artifact layout** every `superclaude eval run`\ninvocation must produce so that:\n\n1. Two operators (or CI shards) replaying the same suite at the same\n   wall-clock instant land on **byte-identical** trees — no drift in\n   path shape, no drift in the per-eval emitter filenames, no drift in\n   the summary.json cross-link strings.\n2. An ERRORED eval **always** carries a Python traceback through both\n   the JSONL event log (live, append-only) and the rendered\n   `summary.json` (post-run, archived). A future refactor that loses\n   the traceback at either layer fails loudly here.\n3. The `summary.json` `evals[]` entries name per-eval artifact paths\n   relative to the run directory — so a reviewer can `cat` a\n   transcript from the summary without re-deriving the FR-G4 layout\n   formula.\n\nThe full end-to-end `superclaude eval run` orchestrator loop is a\nT04.10 forward dependency. This module deliberately anchors the\ncontract at the **layout + Reporter seam**: it builds the FR-G4 tree\nvia the `artifact_layout` helpers directly, materializes per-eval\nfiles, then drives `Reporter.write()`. The T04.10 closure must satisfy\nthe same invariants when it lands; nothing in this test couples to a\nspecific run-loop wiring.\n\n## 2. Reproducibility matrix\n\nThe eight pytest cases in `tests/cli/eval/test_artifact_reproducibility.py`\ncover the five acceptance criteria from phase-4-tasklist.md §T04.20\nplus three reinforcing guards. Cross-reference:\n\n| # | Test function | AC covered | Pins |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | `test_run_dir_matches_fr_g4_pattern` | AC-1: run dir matches `.dev/eval-runs/<ISO>/<run-id>/` | Regex pin on `<output_root>/.dev/eval-runs/YYYY-MM-DD/HHMMSSZ-<8-hex>/`. `parse_run_dir_components` round-trips `(date, run_id)` from the path. |\n| 2 | `test_run_dir_deterministic_for_inputs` | AC-1 + reproducibility invariant | Same `(suite_name, started_at)` → byte-identical run dir. Different `suite_name` OR different `started_at` → different run-id (sha256-deterministic per T04.13). |\n| 3 | `test_per_eval_logs_jsonl_present_and_parsable` | AC-2: `logs.jsonl` exists per eval | The JSONL lives at the pinned filename inside `per-eval/<eval_id>/`, every line is JSON-parseable, the file is non-empty. |\n| 4 | `test_per_eval_tty_transcript_present_and_non_empty` | AC-3: `tty.transcript` exists per eval | `tty.transcript` lands at the pinned filename inside `per-eval/<eval_id>/`, non-empty bytes. |\n| 5 | `test_errored_outcome_records_stack_trace` | AC-4: traceback on ERRORED | A real `RuntimeError` is raised + captured; the resulting traceback appears VERBATIM in both (a) the JSONL `result.failure` event and (b) the rendered `summary.json` `evals[].expects[0].failure.traceback`. |\n| 6 | `test_summary_json_cross_links_per_eval_artifacts` | AC-5: `evals[].artifacts` cross-link | Every `evals[]` row's `artifacts` mapping names `logs.jsonl`, `tty.transcript`, and the `artifacts/` directory; each cited POSIX path resolves to a real file/dir under the run directory. |\n| 7 | `test_artifact_tree_reproducible_across_replays` | AC-1 + AC-5 reinforcing | Two independent replays of the same `(suite_name, started_at, eval set)` produce **byte-identical** `summary.json` outputs (modulo the run-dir prefix), proving the cross-link strings are stable across replays. |\n| 8 | `test_parse_run_dir_rejects_non_layout_paths` | Negative guard | `parse_run_dir_components` raises on paths that do not match the FR-G4 layout — guards against silent acceptance of malformed paths. |\n\n## 3. FR-G4 layout pinned by this module\n\n```\n<output_root>/\n└── .dev/eval-runs/                           # RUN_DIR_PREFIX\n    └── <YYYY-MM-DD>/                         # date component of started_at\n        └── <HHMMSSZ>-<8-hex>/                # run_id = compose_run_id(suite_name, started_at)\n            ├── summary.json                  # Reporter.write target (DM-012)\n            └── per-eval/                     # PER_EVAL_DIRNAME\n                └── <eval_id>/                # one subtree per EvalOutcome\n                    ├── logs.jsonl            # LOGS_JSONL_NAME\n                    ├── tty.transcript        # TTY_TRANSCRIPT_NAME\n                    └── artifacts/            # ARTIFACTS_SUBDIR_NAME\n```\n\nThe `<8-hex>` suffix of the run-id is `sha256(suite_name || ISO-Z-instant)[:8]` —\ndeterministic, so two replays collide on the same path. This is the\nload-bearing reproducibility guarantee tests 1, 2, and 7 exercise.\n\n## 4. Cross-link contract (AC-5 detail)\n\nPer DM-001, each `EvalOutcome.artifacts` is a `Mapping[str, str]`\nwhere:\n- keys are the well-known names `\"logs.jsonl\"`, `\"tty.transcript\"`,\n  `\"artifacts\"`;\n- values are POSIX paths **relative to the run directory** (not\n  absolute, not relative to CWD).\n\nTest 6 verifies the cross-link by resolving every value against the\nrun directory and asserting the resulting path exists. The\n\"relative-to-run-dir\" convention is what makes the artifact tree\n**relocatable**: a CI job can tar the run dir and a reviewer can\nextract it anywhere on disk, and every cross-link in `summary.json`\nstill resolves.\n\n## 5. Stack-trace channel duality (AC-4 detail)\n\nA real Python traceback is generated by raising and catching a\n`RuntimeError` inside the test fixture (rather than hand-crafting a\nmulti-line string), so the test exercises the exact stringification\ncontract production code will hit: `traceback.format_exception(...)`\noutput, line breaks preserved, indentation preserved.\n\nThe test then asserts the **same traceback string** appears in two\nplaces:\n\n1. **JSONL channel** (`logs.jsonl`, the live event log) — the failure\n   event carries `result.failure.traceback`. This is the\n   append-only stream T03.05 / D-0049 emits during the run.\n2. **Summary channel** (`summary.json` `evals[].expects[].failure`)\n   — `ExpectFailure.traceback` (DM-005) carries the same string,\n   serialized by `Reporter.to_json()` post-run.\n\nHolding both pins in one test guards against a future refactor that\ndrops the field at either layer (e.g., truncating the traceback in the\nsummary while keeping it in the JSONL, or vice versa).\n\n## 6. T04.10 forward dependency\n\nThe full `superclaude eval run` loop has not landed (it is T04.10);\nthis module therefore exercises the layout + Reporter contract via\ndirect calls to `compose_run_dir`, `allocate_per_eval_paths`, and\n`Reporter.write()`, materializing per-eval files inside `tmp_path`.\n\nNo tests in this module are skipped — every guard runs today. When\nT04.10 lands, the orchestrator must produce a tree that satisfies the\nsame invariants this module pins. The handoff is asymmetric: T04.10\nwill reuse the same `artifact_layout` helpers (FR-G4 / D-0074), so a\nT04.10 closure that emits these helpers is automatically conformant.\n\n## 7. Acceptance criteria mapping\n\nFrom phase-4-tasklist.md §T04.20:\n\n| AC | Verified by |\n|---|---|\n| File `tests/cli/eval/test_artifact_reproducibility.py` asserts run dir matches `.dev/eval-runs/<ISO>/<run-id>/`. | Test 1 (`test_run_dir_matches_fr_g4_pattern`) + test 2 (`test_run_dir_deterministic_for_inputs`). |\n| Per-eval `logs.jsonl`, `tty.transcript` exist; stack trace recorded on ERRORED status. | Test 3 (`logs.jsonl`), test 4 (`tty.transcript`), test 5 (stack trace on ERRORED — JSONL + summary). |\n| summary.json `evals[]` entries reference per-eval artifact paths. | Test 6 (`test_summary_json_cross_links_per_eval_artifacts`) — each `artifacts[*]` value resolves under the run dir; test 7 (`test_artifact_tree_reproducible_across_replays`) confirms cross-links are byte-stable. |\n| `TASKLIST_ROOT/artifacts/D-0080/spec.md` records the reproducibility matrix. | This file. |\n| Evidence saved under `TASKLIST_ROOT/evidence/T04.20/`. | `.dev/releases/current/cliEval/evidence/T04.20/test-output.txt` — pytest log, 8 passed, 0 skipped. |\n\n## 8. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01V7cnD1TScQLwapm5wTDku7","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"TEST-008 — process-boundary exit-code semantics for ``superclaude eval run``.\n2\t\n3\tT04.19 / D-0079 — pins the four exit codes named in design-spec §4 at the\n4\t**process boundary** (via ``subprocess.run`` + ``returncode``), the\n5\tsingle surface every operator, CI job, and downstream pipeline reads.\n6\t\n7\tThe four exit codes (design-spec §4):\n8\t\n9\t* ``0`` — clean: every expanded eval reached PASS / SKIPPED / XFAIL and\n10\t  no breach occurred.\n11\t* ``1`` — failing run: at least one eval ended FAIL / ERRORED / TIMEOUT\n12\t  / XPASS but the harness itself ran to completion.\n13\t* ``2`` — harness contract error: invalid flag, scratch-root violation,\n14\t  suite-not-found, disk-budget exceeded, Reporter contract violation,\n15\t  capability gate breach, etc. The harness rejected the run before /\n16\t  during dispatch.\n17\t* ``3`` — interrupted: SIGINT / SIGTERM landed mid-run and the\n18\t  cooperative cancellation path drained.\n19\t\n20\tSibling tests:\n21\t\n22\t* :mod:`tests.cli.eval.test_reporter_contract` (T04.17) pins the same\n23\t  exit-code-2 contract at the *library* boundary\n24\t  (``REPORTER_CONTRACT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE`` constant + exception\n25\t  type). {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01AyQTS8Yc89yRsJruhsdswX","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"NFR-PERF4 disk-budget poller (T03.19 / D-0060 / R-060).\n2\t\n3\tDesign-spec §4 (``--max-disk-mb``) and §13 (NFR-PERF4) require the\n4\torchestrator to bound the cumulative artifact size under\n5\t``--output-dir`` during a run. The motivation is the R4 risk row in\n6\tdesign-spec §10: a fan-out of 15 concurrent HOMEs with PTY transcripts\n7\tcan grow past several hundred megabytes on a slow eval; left unchecked,\n8\tthat fills the developer host (or a CI runner with a tight disk quota)\n9\tand corrupts every neighbouring run. The orchestrator addresses this\n10\tby polling the run-directory size every five seconds and signalling a\n11\t\"stop scheduling, drain in-flight, exit 2\" breach when the configured\n12\tbudget is exceeded.\n13\t\n14\tThis module implements the polling primitive. The integration with\n15\t:class:`RunOrchestrator` lives in ``orchestrator.py``; the CLI flag\n16\t(``--max-disk-mb``) is wired by the Phase 4 dispatcher per design-spec\n17\t§4. The poller is deliberately decoupled from both so it can be exercised\n18\tin isolation by the T03.19 fixture suite (``test_disk_budget.py``) and\n19\tre-used by any future component (eg. the doctor pre-flight) that needs\n20\ta cheap, side-effect-free disk gauge.\n21\t\n22\tPublic surface\n23\t==============\n24\t\n25\t:class:`DiskBudgetPoller`\n26\t    Background-thread poller. Constructed with an output directory and a\n27\t    ``max_disk_mb`` budget; ``start()`` / ``stop()`` (or the context-\n28\t    manager form) drive the background thread. Exposes :meth:`is_breached`\n29\t    so the orchestrator can short-circuit its submission loop and\n30\t    :meth:`breach_detail` for the partial-summary payload.\n31\t\n32\t:data:`DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_EXIT_CODE`\n33\t    Process exit code emitted by the Phase 4 dispatcher when the poller\n34\t    has flagged a breach. ``2`` per design-spec §4 (harness error).\n35\t\n36\t:data:`DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME`\n37\t    Filename of the side-car the poller writes inside the run directory\n38\t    on breach. The Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) surfaces this path under\n39\t    ``RunSummary.artifacts['disk_budget_exceeded']``; the filename is\n40\t    pinned here so consumers do not have to discover it by scan.\n41\t\n42\tBreach semantics\n43\t================\n44\t\n45\tWhen ``max_disk_mb > 0``:\n46\t\n47\t* The poller measures cumulative size of the output directory tree every\n48\t  ``tick_sec`` seconds (default ``5.0``). The first tick fires *after*\n49\t  the initial sleep so an empty run directory does not get flagged on\n50\t  start-up.\n51\t* When measured usage exceeds ``max_disk_mb * 1024 * 1024`` bytes, the\n52\t  poller writes ``DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME`` into the output\n53\t  directory, sets its internal ``is_breached()`` flag, and exits the\n54\t  loop. The flag is one-shot — once set, the poller stays in the\n55\t  breached state for the rest of its lifetime.\n56\t* The orchestrator polls ``is_breached()`` between submissions; on a\n57\t  breach it stops scheduling new specs and synthesises a\n58\t  ``SKIPPED`` outcome (skip_reason ``\"disk_budget_exceeded\"``) for every\n59\t  unsubmitted spec. In-flight workers continue to completion — the\n60\t  poller never kills a thread or sends a signal.\n61\t\n62\tWhen ``max_disk_mb == 0``:\n63\t\n64\t* The poller is *disabled*. ``start()`` is a no-op, the background\n65\t  thread is never spawned, and :meth:`is_breached` always returns\n66\t  ``False``. The orchestrator behaves as if no poller were wired.\n67\t  Design-spec §4 documents ``--max-disk-mb 0`` as the \"disable\"\n68\t  invocation.\n69\t\n70\tThread safety\n71\t=============\n72\t\n73\tThe breach state is stored as a :class:`threading.Event`. Reading the\n74\tdetail mapping after :meth:`is_breached` returns ``True`` is safe because\n75\tthe detail is published *before* the event is set (release-then-set\n76\tordering). The poller thread is the only writer of the detail; the\n77\torchestrator (main) thread is the only reader. Both ends communicate\n78\tthrough the event without explicit locking.\n79\t\"\"\"\n80\t\n81\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n82\t\n83\timport json\n84\timport threading\n85\timport time\n86\tfrom dataclasses import dataclass\n87\tfrom pathlib import Path\n88\tfrom typing import Mapping, Optional\n89\t\n90\t__all__ = [\n91\t    \"DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME\",\n92\t    \"DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_EXIT_CODE\",\n93\t    \"DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_REASON\",\n94\t    \"DEFAULT_DISK_BUDGET_MB\",\n95\t    \"DEFAULT_DISK_POLL_TICK_SEC\",\n96\t    \"DiskBudgetPoller\",\n97\t]\n98\t\n99\t\n100\t# Process exit code returned by the Phase 4 CLI dispatcher when the\n101\t# poller has signalled a breach. The disk-budget breach is a harness-\n103\t# enforced abort, not a per-eval failure, so it uses the same code as\n104\t# the loader / scratch-root / reporter-contract violations.\n105\tDISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_EXIT_CODE: int = 2\n106\t\n107\t# Filename the poller writes when a breach is detected. The motivation is the R4 risk row in\ndesign-spec §10: a fan-out of 15 concurrent HOMEs with PTY transcripts\ncan grow past several hundred megabytes on a slow eval; left unchecked,\nthat fills the developer host (or a CI runner with a tight disk quota)\nand corrupts every neighbouring run. The orchestrator addresses this\nby polling the run-directory size every five seconds and signalling a\n\"stop scheduling, drain in-flight, exit 2\" breach when the configured\nbudget is exceeded.\n\nThis module implements the polling primitive. The integration with\n:class:`RunOrchestrator` lives in ``orchestrator.py``; the CLI flag\n(``--max-disk-mb``) is wired by the Phase 4 dispatcher per design-spec\n§4. The poller is deliberately decoupled from both so it can be exercised\nin isolation by the T03.19 fixture suite (``test_disk_budget.py``) and\nre-used by any future component (eg. the doctor pre-flight) that needs\na cheap, side-effect-free disk gauge.\n\nPublic surface\n==============\n\n:class:`DiskBudgetPoller`\n    Background-thread poller. Constructed with an output directory and a\n    ``max_disk_mb`` budget; ``start()`` / ``stop()`` (or the context-\n    manager form) drive the background thread. Exposes :meth:`is_breached`\n    so the orchestrator can short-circuit its submission loop and\n    :meth:`breach_detail` for the partial-summary payload.\n\n:data:`DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_EXIT_CODE`\n    Process exit code emitted by the Phase 4 dispatcher when the poller\n    has flagged a breach. ``2`` per design-spec §4 (harness error).\n\n:data:`DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME`\n    Filename of the side-car the poller writes inside the run directory\n    on breach. The Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) surfaces this path under\n    ``RunSummary.artifacts['disk_budget_exceeded']``; the filename is\n    pinned here so consumers do not have to discover it by scan.\n\nBreach semantics\n================\n\nWhen ``max_disk_mb > 0``:\n\n* The poller measures cumulative size of the output directory tree every\n  ``tick_sec`` seconds (default ``5.0``). The first tick fires *after*\n  the initial sleep so an empty run directory does not get flagged on\n  start-up.\n* When measured usage exceeds ``max_disk_mb * 1024 * 1024`` bytes, the\n  poller writes ``DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME`` into the output\n  directory, sets its internal ``is_breached()`` flag, and exits the\n  loop. The flag is one-shot — once set, the poller stays in the\n  breached state for the rest of its lifetime.\n* The orchestrator polls ``is_breached()`` between submissions; on a\n  breach it stops scheduling new specs and synthesises a\n  ``SKIPPED`` outcome (skip_reason ``\"disk_budget_exceeded\"``) for every\n  unsubmitted spec. In-flight workers continue to completion — the\n  poller never kills a thread or sends a signal.\n\nWhen ``max_disk_mb == 0``:\n\n* The poller is *disabled*. ``start()`` is a no-op, the background\n  thread is never spawned, and :meth:`is_breached` always returns\n  ``False``. The orchestrator behaves as if no poller were wired.\n  Design-spec §4 documents ``--max-disk-mb 0`` as the \"disable\"\n  invocation.\n\nThread safety\n=============\n\nThe breach state is stored as a :class:`threading.Event`. Reading the\ndetail mapping after :meth:`is_breached` returns ``True`` is safe because\nthe detail is published *before* the event is set (release-then-set\nordering). The poller thread is the only writer of the detail; the\norchestrator (main) thread is the only reader. Both ends communicate\nthrough the event without explicit locking.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nimport threading\nimport time\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Mapping, Optional\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME\",\n    \"DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_EXIT_CODE\",\n    \"DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_REASON\",\n    \"DEFAULT_DISK_BUDGET_MB\",\n    \"DEFAULT_DISK_POLL_TICK_SEC\",\n    \"DiskBudgetPoller\",\n]\n\n\n# Process exit code returned by the Phase 4 CLI dispatcher when the\n# poller has signalled a breach. Matches the design-spec §4 exit-code\n# table: ``2`` = harness error. The disk-budget breach is a harness-\n# enforced abort, not a per-eval failure, so it uses the same code as\n# the loader / scratch-root / reporter-contract violations.\nDISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_EXIT_CODE: int = 2\n\n# Filename the poller writes when a breach is detected. Pinned here so\n# the orchestrator, Reporter, and integration tests all reference one\n# constant. ``.json`` because the payload is a structured\n# :class:`BreachDetail` projection.\nDISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME: str = \"disk_budget_exceeded.json\"\n\n# ``skip_reason`` token written into every synthesised SKIPPED outcome\n# the orchestrator emits for unsubmitted specs after a breach. The Reporter surfaces this dict back through\n    ``RunSummary.artifacts['disk_budget_exceeded']`` so reviewers can\n    diagnose \"why did the run exit 2\" without re-running the poller.\n\n    Attributes\n    ----------\n    reason:\n        Always :data:`DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_REASON`. Created if\n        missing only when ``ensure_output_dir=True``; otherwise the\n        first tick on a non-existent directory measures ``0`` bytes.\n    max_disk_mb:\n        Budget in megabytes. ``0`` disables the poller entirely (see\n        module docstring). got {type(max_disk_mb).__name__}\"\n            )\n        if max_disk_mb < 0:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"max_disk_mb must be >= 0; got {max_disk_mb} \"\n                \"(use 0 to disable the budget)\"\n            )\n        if tick_sec <= 0:\n            raise ValueError(f\"tick_sec must be > 0; The orchestrator polls this between submissions\n        to decide whether to keep scheduling.\n        \"\"\"\n        return self._breach_event.is_set()\n\n    def breach_detail(self) -> Optional[Mapping[str, object]]:\n        \"\"\"Return the breach payload as a plain dict, or ``None``.\n\n        The mapping mirrors :class:`BreachDetail.to_dict`. Cross-link contract (AC-5 detail)\n\nPer DM-001, each `EvalOutcome.artifacts` is a `Mapping[str, str]`\nwhere:\n- keys are the well-known names `\"logs.jsonl\"`, `\"tty.transcript\"`,\n  `\"artifacts\"`;\n- values are POSIX paths **relative to the run directory** (not\n  absolute, not relative to CWD).\n\nTest 6 verifies the cross-link by resolving every value against the\nrun directory and asserting the resulting path exists. The\n\"relative-to-run-dir\" convention is what makes the artifact tree\n**relocatable**: a CI job can tar the run dir and a reviewer can\nextract it anywhere on disk, and every cross-link in `summary.json`\nstill resolves.\n\n## 5. Stack-trace channel duality (AC-4 detail)\n\nA real Python traceback is generated by raising and catching a\n`RuntimeError` inside the test fixture (rather than hand-crafting a\nmulti-line string), so the test exercises the exact stringification\ncontract production code will hit: `traceback.format_exception(...)`\noutput, line breaks preserved, indentation preserved.\n\nThe test then asserts the **same traceback string** appears in two\nplaces:\n\n1. **JSONL channel** (`logs.jsonl`, the live event log) — the failure\n   event carries `result.failure.traceback`. This is the\n   append-only stream T03.05 / D-0049 emits during the run.\n2. **Summary channel** (`summary.json` `evals[].expects[].failure`)\n   — `ExpectFailure.traceback` (DM-005) carries the same string,\n   serialized by `Reporter.to_json()` post-run.\n\nHolding both pins in one test guards against a future refactor that\ndrops the field at either layer (e.g., truncating the traceback in the\nsummary while keeping it in the JSONL, or vice versa).\n\n## 6. T04.10 forward dependency\n\nThe full `superclaude eval run` loop has not landed (it is T04.10);\nthis module therefore exercises the layout + Reporter contract via\ndirect calls to `compose_run_dir`, `allocate_per_eval_paths`, and\n`Reporter.write()`, materializing per-eval files inside `tmp_path`.\n\nNo tests in this module are skipped — every guard runs today. When\nT04.10 lands, the orchestrator must produce a tree that satisfies the\nsame invariants this module pins. The handoff is asymmetric: T04.10\nwill reuse the same `artifact_layout` helpers (FR-G4 / D-0074), so a\nT04.10 closure that emits these helpers is automatically conformant.\n\n## 7. Acceptance criteria mapping\n\nFrom phase-4-tasklist.md §T04.20:\n\n| AC | Verified by |\n|---|---|\n| File `tests/cli/eval/test_artifact_reproducibility.py` asserts run dir matches `.dev/eval-runs/<ISO>/<run-id>/`. | Test 1 (`test_run_dir_matches_fr_g4_pattern`) + test 2 (`test_run_dir_deterministic_for_inputs`). |\n| Per-eval `logs.jsonl`, `tty.transcript` exist; stack trace recorded on ERRORED status. | Test 3 (`logs.jsonl`), test 4 (`tty.transcript`), test 5 (stack trace on ERRORED — JSONL + summary). |\n| summary.json `evals[]` entries reference per-eval artifact paths. | Test 6 (`test_summary_json_cross_links_per_eval_artifacts`) — each `artifacts[*]` value resolves under the run dir; test 7 (`test_artifact_tree_reproducible_across_replays`) confirms cross-links are byte-stable. |\n| `TASKLIST_ROOT/artifacts/D-0080/spec.md` records the reproducibility matrix. | This file. |\n| Evidence saved under `TASKLIST_ROOT/evidence/T04.20/`. | `.dev/releases/current/cliEval/evidence/T04.20/test-output.txt` — pytest log, 8 passed, 0 skipped. |\n\n## 8. Cross-links\n\n* FR-G4 / R-074 / D-0074 / T04.13 (`artifact_layout.py`) — the layout\n  source-of-truth this module exercises directly. T04.13's\n  `test_artifact_layout.py` pins the helper contract in isolation; this\n  module pins the **end-to-end** layout + cross-link + traceback\n  contract on top of those helpers.\n* DM-001 / T01.12 (`EvalOutcome.artifacts`) — the cross-link field\n  test 6 reads.\n* DM-005 / T01.16 (`ExpectFailure.traceback`) — the traceback channel\n  test 5 reads.\n* DM-012 / T03.10 (`summary.schema.json`) — the schema the Reporter\n  renders to; `evals[].artifacts` and\n  `evals[].expects[].failure.traceback` are both pinned by §6.x of the\n  schema and read by tests 5 and 6.\n* COMP-008 / T03.11 (`Reporter`) — the post-run renderer; this module\n  drives `Reporter.write()` to materialize `summary.json` and assert on\n  the rendered bytes.\n* T04.17 / D-0078 (`test_reporter_contract.py`) — sibling test that\n  pins the FR-RPT1 N'-vs-K dimensional invariant on the Reporter; this\n  module trusts that guard and focuses on the artifact + cross-link\n  contract.\n* T04.10 / D-0072 (forward dep) — the `eval_run` orchestrator closure;\n  when it lands, the run loop must produce a tree conformant to the\n  invariants pinned here.\n","numLines":169,"startLine":1,"totalLines":169}}}
