The motivation is the R4 risk row in\ndesign-spec §10: a fan-out of 15 concurrent HOMEs with PTY transcripts\ncan grow past several hundred megabytes on a slow eval; left unchecked,\nthat fills the developer host (or a CI runner with a tight disk quota)\nand corrupts every neighbouring run. The orchestrator addresses this\nby polling the run-directory size every five seconds and signalling a\n\"stop scheduling, drain in-flight, exit 2\" breach when the configured\nbudget is exceeded.\n\nThis module implements the polling primitive. The integration with\n:class:`RunOrchestrator` lives in ``orchestrator.py``; the CLI flag\n(``--max-disk-mb``) is wired by the Phase 4 dispatcher per design-spec\n§4. The poller is deliberately decoupled from both so it can be exercised\nin isolation by the T03.19 fixture suite (``test_disk_budget.py``) and\nre-used by any future component (eg. the doctor pre-flight) that needs\na cheap, side-effect-free disk gauge.\n\nPublic surface\n==============\n\n:class:`DiskBudgetPoller`\n    Background-thread poller. Constructed with an output directory and a\n    ``max_disk_mb`` budget; ``start()`` / ``stop()`` (or the context-\n    manager form) drive the background thread. Exposes :meth:`is_breached`\n    so the orchestrator can short-circuit its submission loop and\n    :meth:`breach_detail` for the partial-summary payload.\n\n:data:`DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_EXIT_CODE`\n    Process exit code emitted by the Phase 4 dispatcher when the poller\n    has flagged a breach. ``2`` per design-spec §4 (harness error).\n\n:data:`DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME`\n    Filename of the side-car the poller writes inside the run directory\n    on breach. The Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) surfaces this path under\n    ``RunSummary.artifacts['disk_budget_exceeded']``; the filename is\n    pinned here so consumers do not have to discover it by scan.\n\nBreach semantics\n================\n\nWhen ``max_disk_mb > 0``:\n\n* The poller measures cumulative size of the output directory tree every\n  ``tick_sec`` seconds (default ``5.0``). The first tick fires *after*\n  the initial sleep so an empty run directory does not get flagged on\n  start-up.\n* When measured usage exceeds ``max_disk_mb * 1024 * 1024`` bytes, the\n  poller writes ``DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME`` into the output\n  directory, sets its internal ``is_breached()`` flag, and exits the\n  loop. The flag is one-shot — once set, the poller stays in the\n  breached state for the rest of its lifetime.\n* The orchestrator polls ``is_breached()`` between submissions; on a\n  breach it stops scheduling new specs and synthesises a\n  ``SKIPPED`` outcome (skip_reason ``\"disk_budget_exceeded\"``) for every\n  unsubmitted spec. In-flight workers continue to completion — the\n  poller never kills a thread or sends a signal.\n\nWhen ``max_disk_mb == 0``:\n\n* The poller is *disabled*. ``start()`` is a no-op, the background\n  thread is never spawned, and :meth:`is_breached` always returns\n  ``False``. The orchestrator behaves as if no poller were wired.\n  Design-spec §4 documents ``--max-disk-mb 0`` as the \"disable\"\n  invocation.\n\nThread safety\n=============\n\nThe breach state is stored as a :class:`threading.Event`. Reading the\ndetail mapping after :meth:`is_breached` returns ``True`` is safe because\nthe detail is published *before* the event is set (release-then-set\nordering). The poller thread is the only writer of the detail; the\norchestrator (main) thread is the only reader. Both ends communicate\nthrough the event without explicit locking.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nimport threading\nimport time\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Mapping, Optional\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME\",\n    \"DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_EXIT_CODE\",\n    \"DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_REASON\",\n    \"DEFAULT_DISK_BUDGET_MB\",\n    \"DEFAULT_DISK_POLL_TICK_SEC\",\n    \"DiskBudgetPoller\",\n]\n\n\n# Process exit code returned by the Phase 4 CLI dispatcher when the\n# poller has signalled a breach. Matches the design-spec §4 exit-code\n# table: ``2`` = harness error. The disk-budget breach is a harness-\n# enforced abort, not a per-eval failure, so it uses the same code as\n# the loader / scratch-root / reporter-contract violations.\nDISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_EXIT_CODE: int = 2\n\n# Filename the poller writes when a breach is detected. Pinned here so\n# the orchestrator, Reporter, and integration tests all reference one\n# constant. ``.json`` because the payload is a structured\n# :class:`BreachDetail` projection.\nDISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME: str = \"disk_budget_exceeded.json\"\n\n# ``skip_reason`` token written into every synthesised SKIPPED outcome\n# the orchestrator emits for unsubmitted specs after a breach. Stable\n# value so the Reporter / CLI consumers can match on it.\nDISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_REASON: str = \"disk_budget_exceeded\"\n\n# Design-spec §4 default budget. The CLI flag default mirrors this so a\n# bare ``superclaude eval run`` is bounded out of the box.\nDEFAULT_DISK_BUDGET_MB: int = 1024\n\n# Design-spec §13 polling cadence. Five seconds is a coarse-enough tick\n# that the recursive ``stat()`` walk does not become a measurable share\n# of runtime even for a 15-eval suite emitting transcripts at a few MB/s.\nDEFAULT_DISK_POLL_TICK_SEC: float = 5.0\n\n\n_BYTES_PER_MB: int = 1024 * 1024\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass BreachDetail:\n    \"\"\"Side-car payload describing a disk-budget breach.\n\n    Serialised verbatim into :data:`DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME`\n    by the poller. The Reporter surfaces this dict back through\n    ``RunSummary.artifacts['disk_budget_exceeded']`` so reviewers can\n    diagnose \"why did the run exit 2\" without re-running the poller.\n\n    Attributes\n    ----------\n    reason:\n        Always :data:`DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_REASON`. Pinned so consumers\n        can match on the constant token without parsing structure.\n    output_dir:\n        Absolute path of the directory the poller was watching, as a\n        string (paths do not JSON-serialise without a converter).\n    usage_bytes:\n        Measured cumulative size of the directory tree at the breach\n        tick. ``int`` so the value round-trips through JSON losslessly.\n    budget_bytes:\n        Effective budget in bytes (``max_disk_mb * 1024 * 1024``).\n    max_disk_mb:\n        Original budget the operator configured, in megabytes. Stored\n        alongside ``budget_bytes`` so the side-car is readable without a\n        mental conversion.\n    ticked_at:\n        ISO-8601 timestamp of the breach tick. Stamped via\n        :func:`time.gmtime` + :func:`time.strftime` so the value is\n        timezone-stable (UTC ``Z``).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    reason: str\n    output_dir: str\n    usage_bytes: int\n    budget_bytes: int\n    max_disk_mb: int\n    ticked_at: str\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict:\n        \"\"\"Return a JSON-serialisable dict in field-declared order.\"\"\"\n        return {\n            \"reason\": self.reason,\n            \"output_dir\": self.output_dir,\n            \"usage_bytes\": self.usage_bytes,\n            \"budget_bytes\": self.budget_bytes,\n            \"max_disk_mb\": self.max_disk_mb,\n            \"ticked_at\": self.ticked_at,\n        }\n\n\nclass DiskBudgetPoller:\n    \"\"\"Background-thread poller enforcing the NFR-PERF4 disk budget.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    output_dir:\n        Directory whose recursive size is summed every tick. Created if\n        missing only when ``ensure_output_dir=True``; otherwise the\n        first tick on a non-existent directory measures ``0`` bytes.\n    max_disk_mb:\n        Budget in megabytes. ``0`` disables the poller entirely (see\n        module docstring). Negative values raise :class:`ValueError`.\n    tick_sec:\n        Seconds between size measurements. Defaults to\n        :data:`DEFAULT_DISK_POLL_TICK_SEC` (5 s). Tests pass a small\n        value (eg. 0.05) to keep the suite fast.\n    artifact_name:\n        Filename of the breach side-car. Defaults to\n        :data:`DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME`. Tests substitute a\n        unique name when several pollers share a directory.\n    clock:\n        Optional ``() -> str`` returning the ``ticked_at`` ISO-8601\n        timestamp. Tests inject a deterministic clock; the default\n        formatter uses :func:`time.gmtime` so production output is\n        timezone-stable.\n\n    Notes\n    -----\n    The recursive size walk uses :meth:`pathlib.Path.rglob` and\n    :meth:`pathlib.Path.stat` with ``follow_symlinks=False`` so the\n    poller cannot be tricked into double-counting via a symlink into a\n    sibling tree. Files that disappear between the directory walk and\n    the ``stat()`` call (eg. an in-flight runner cleaning up its scratch\n    HOME) are silently ignored — a transient OS error must not crash the\n    poller thread.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    DEFAULT_TICK_SEC = DEFAULT_DISK_POLL_TICK_SEC\n    DEFAULT_MAX_DISK_MB = DEFAULT_DISK_BUDGET_MB\n    ARTIFACT_NAME = DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME\n    BREACH_REASON = DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_REASON\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        output_dir: Path,\n        *,\n        max_disk_mb: int = DEFAULT_DISK_BUDGET_MB,\n        tick_sec: float = DEFAULT_DISK_POLL_TICK_SEC,\n        artifact_name: str = DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME,\n        ensure_output_dir: bool = False,\n        clock: Optional[\"callable[[], str]\"] = None,\n    ) -> None:\n        if not isinstance(max_disk_mb, int) or isinstance(max_disk_mb, bool):\n            raise TypeError(\n                f\"max_disk_mb must be int; got {type(max_disk_mb).__name__}\"\n            )\n        if max_disk_mb < 0:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"max_disk_mb must be >= 0; got {max_disk_mb} \"\n                \"(use 0 to disable the budget)\"\n            )\n        if tick_sec <= 0:\n            raise ValueError(f\"tick_sec must be > 0; got {tick_sec}\")\n        if not artifact_name:\n            raise ValueError(\"artifact_name must be non-empty\")\n\n        self._output_dir = Path(output_dir)\n        if ensure_output_dir:\n            self._output_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n\n        self._max_disk_mb = int(max_disk_mb)\n        self._budget_bytes = int(max_disk_mb) * _BYTES_PER_MB\n        self._tick_sec = float(tick_sec)\n        self._artifact_name = artifact_name\n        self._clock = clock or _default_iso_clock\n\n        self._stop_event = threading.Event()\n        self._breach_event = threading.Event()\n        self._detail: Optional[BreachDetail] = None\n        self._artifact_path: Optional[Path] = None\n        self._thread: Optional[threading.Thread] = None\n        self._lifecycle_lock = threading.Lock()\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Read-only inspection surface\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    @property\n    def enabled(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"``True`` when the poller will actually spawn a thread.\"\"\"\n        return self._max_disk_mb > 0\n\n    @property\n    def max_disk_mb(self) -> int:\n        return self._max_disk_mb\n\n    @property\n    def budget_bytes(self) -> int:\n        return self._budget_bytes\n\n    @property\n    def tick_sec(self) -> float:\n        return self._tick_sec\n\n    @property\n    def output_dir(self) -> Path:\n        return self._output_dir\n\n    @property\n    def artifact_name(self) -> str:\n        return self._artifact_name\n\n    def is_breached(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"``True`` once a breach has been recorded.\n\n        One-shot — once the flag flips it never resets for the lifetime\n        of the poller. The orchestrator polls this between submissions\n        to decide whether to keep scheduling.\n        \"\"\"\n        return self._breach_event.is_set()\n\n    def breach_detail(self) -> Optional[Mapping[str, object]]:\n        \"\"\"Return the breach payload as a plain dict, or ``None``.\n\n        The mapping mirrors :class:`BreachDetail.to_dict`. Returns\n        ``None`` until :meth:`is_breached` reports ``True``.\n        \"\"\"\n        detail = self._detail\n        if detail is None:\n            return None\n        return detail.to_dict()\n\n    def artifact_path(self) -> Optional[Path]:\n        \"\"\"Path of the side-car file the poller wrote, or ``None``.\n\n        Equal to ``output_dir / artifact_name`` once a breach has been\n        recorded; ``None`` while the poller is healthy.\n        \"\"\"\n        return self._artifact_path\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Lifecycle\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def start(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Spawn the background polling thread.\n\n        No-op when :attr:`enabled` is ``False`` (``max_disk_mb=0``) or\n        when the thread is already running. Idempotent so the\n        orchestrator can call :meth:`start` unconditionally without\n        guarding the disabled case.\n        \"\"\"\n        with self._lifecycle_lock:\n            if not self.enabled:\n                return\n            if self._thread is not None and self._thread.is_alive():\n                return\n            self._stop_event.clear()\n            self._thread = threading.Thread(\n                target=self._poll_loop,\n                name=f\"cliEval-disk-budget-{self._max_disk_mb}MB\",\n                daemon=True,\n            )\n            self._thread.start()\n\n    def stop(self, *, timeout: Optional[float] = None) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Signal the background thread to exit and join it.\n\n        ``timeout`` is forwarded to :meth:`threading.Thread.join`. The\n        method is idempotent and safe to call from any thread; signal-\n        handler installers MAY call it from the main thread without\n        knowing whether the poller was ever started.\n        \"\"\"\n        with self._lifecycle_lock:\n            thread = self._thread\n            self._stop_event.set()\n        if thread is not None:\n            thread.join(timeout=timeout)\n\n    def __enter__(self) -> \"DiskBudgetPoller\":\n        self.start()\n        return self\n\n    def __exit__(self, exc_type, exc, tb) -> None:\n        self.stop()\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Polling internals\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _poll_loop(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Tick-driven measurement loop.\n\n        Uses :meth:`threading.Event.wait` rather than :func:`time.sleep`\n        so :meth:`stop` can shortcut the next tick immediately — the\n        orchestrator does not want to wait up to ``tick_sec`` seconds\n        for the poller to notice it is being shut down.\n        \"\"\"\n        while not self._stop_event.wait(self._tick_sec):\n            usage_bytes = self._compute_usage()\n            if usage_bytes > self._budget_bytes:\n                self._record_breach(usage_bytes)\n                return\n\n    def _compute_usage(self) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Sum every regular file under ``output_dir`` non-recursively-of-symlinks.\n\n        Returns ``0`` when the directory does not exist yet; lets the\n        poller run before the orchestrator has created its run\n        directory without raising. Per-file ``OSError`` is swallowed so\n        a vanishing in-flight scratch file does not crash the poller.\n        \"\"\"\n        root = self._output_dir\n        if not root.is_dir():\n            return 0\n        total = 0\n        # ``rglob('*')`` returns every entry including directories and\n        # symlinks. We only count regular files (``is_file()`` returns\n        # ``False`` for symlinks to files thanks to ``follow_symlinks``\n        # behaviour of the underlying ``stat`` — but we also guard with\n        # ``is_symlink()`` to be explicit about the policy).\n        for entry in root.rglob(\"*\"):\n            if entry.is_symlink():\n                continue\n            try:\n                if not entry.is_file():\n                    continue\n                total += entry.stat().st_size\n            except (OSError, FileNotFoundError):\n                # Transient: file was reaped between ``rglob`` and\n                # ``stat``. Skip — the next tick will catch any\n                # persistent growth.\n                continue\n        return total\n\n    def _record_breach(self, usage_bytes: int) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Persist the side-car and flip the breach flag.\n\n        The detail is built *before* the side-car is written so a\n        partial-write failure (eg. directory was deleted out from under\n        us) still leaves a valid in-memory payload for the Reporter.\n        The breach flag is set *after* the detail is published so any\n        consumer that reads :meth:`breach_detail` after observing\n        :meth:`is_breached` ``True`` is guaranteed a non-``None``\n        return.\n        \"\"\"\n        detail = BreachDetail(\n            reason=self.BREACH_REASON,\n            output_dir=str(self._output_dir),\n            usage_bytes=int(usage_bytes),\n            budget_bytes=self._budget_bytes,\n            max_disk_mb=self._max_disk_mb,\n            ticked_at=self._clock(),\n        )\n        artifact_path = self._output_dir / self._artifact_name\n        try:\n            self._output_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n            artifact_path.write_text(\n                json.dumps(detail.to_dict(), indent=2, sort_keys=True) + \"\\n\",\n                encoding=\"utf-8\",\n            )\n        except OSError:\n            # Filesystem unavailable — record the breach in memory only\n            # so the orchestrator still observes the flag. The side-car\n            # absence is a known degraded mode documented in the spec.\n            artifact_path = None\n\n        # Publish detail before flipping the flag (release-then-set\n        # ordering — see module docstring).\n        self._detail = detail\n        self._artifact_path = artifact_path\n        self._breach_event.set()\n\n\ndef _default_iso_clock() -> str:\n    \"\"\"Return the current UTC time as an ISO-8601 ``Z`` string.\n\n    Centralised so :class:`DiskBudgetPoller` and the side-car payload\n    use a single timestamp format. Tests inject a deterministic clock\n    via the constructor so we never have to mock ``time``.\n    \"\"\"\n    return time.strftime(\"%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ\", time.gmtime())\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":91,"oldLines":6,"newStart":91,"newLines":7,"lines":["     \"DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME\",","     \"DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_EXIT_CODE\",","     \"DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_REASON\",","+    \"DISK_BUDGET_RETENTION_ADVICE\",","     \"DEFAULT_DISK_BUDGET_MB\",","     \"DEFAULT_DISK_POLL_TICK_SEC\",","     \"DiskBudgetPoller\","]}],"userModified":false,"replaceAll":false}}

The motivation is the R4 risk row in\ndesign-spec §10: a fan-out of 15 concurrent HOMEs with PTY transcripts\ncan grow past several hundred megabytes on a slow eval; left unchecked,\nthat fills the developer host (or a CI runner with a tight disk quota)\nand corrupts every neighbouring run. The orchestrator addresses this\nby polling the run-directory size every five seconds and signalling a\n\"stop scheduling, drain in-flight, exit 2\" breach when the configured\nbudget is exceeded.\n\nThis module implements the polling primitive. The integration with\n:class:`RunOrchestrator` lives in ``orchestrator.py``; the CLI flag\n(``--max-disk-mb``) is wired by the Phase 4 dispatcher per design-spec\n§4. The poller is deliberately decoupled from both so it can be exercised\nin isolation by the T03.19 fixture suite (``test_disk_budget.py``) and\nre-used by any future component (eg. the doctor pre-flight) that needs\na cheap, side-effect-free disk gauge.\n\nPublic surface\n==============\n\n:class:`DiskBudgetPoller`\n    Background-thread poller. Constructed with an output directory and a\n    ``max_disk_mb`` budget; ``start()`` / ``stop()`` (or the context-\n    manager form) drive the background thread. Exposes :meth:`is_breached`\n    so the orchestrator can short-circuit its submission loop and\n    :meth:`breach_detail` for the partial-summary payload.\n\n:data:`DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_EXIT_CODE`\n    Process exit code emitted by the Phase 4 dispatcher when the poller\n    has flagged a breach. ``2`` per design-spec §4 (harness error).\n\n:data:`DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME`\n    Filename of the side-car the poller writes inside the run directory\n    on breach. The Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) surfaces this path under\n    ``RunSummary.artifacts['disk_budget_exceeded']``; the filename is\n    pinned here so consumers do not have to discover it by scan.\n\nBreach semantics\n================\n\nWhen ``max_disk_mb > 0``:\n\n* The poller measures cumulative size of the output directory tree every\n  ``tick_sec`` seconds (default ``5.0``). The first tick fires *after*\n  the initial sleep so an empty run directory does not get flagged on\n  start-up.\n* When measured usage exceeds ``max_disk_mb * 1024 * 1024`` bytes, the\n  poller writes ``DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME`` into the output\n  directory, sets its internal ``is_breached()`` flag, and exits the\n  loop. The flag is one-shot — once set, the poller stays in the\n  breached state for the rest of its lifetime.\n* The orchestrator polls ``is_breached()`` between submissions; on a\n  breach it stops scheduling new specs and synthesises a\n  ``SKIPPED`` outcome (skip_reason ``\"disk_budget_exceeded\"``) for every\n  unsubmitted spec. In-flight workers continue to completion — the\n  poller never kills a thread or sends a signal.\n\nWhen ``max_disk_mb == 0``:\n\n* The poller is *disabled*. ``start()`` is a no-op, the background\n  thread is never spawned, and :meth:`is_breached` always returns\n  ``False``. The orchestrator behaves as if no poller were wired.\n  Design-spec §4 documents ``--max-disk-mb 0`` as the \"disable\"\n  invocation.\n\nThread safety\n=============\n\nThe breach state is stored as a :class:`threading.Event`. Reading the\ndetail mapping after :meth:`is_breached` returns ``True`` is safe because\nthe detail is published *before* the event is set (release-then-set\nordering). The poller thread is the only writer of the detail; the\norchestrator (main) thread is the only reader. Both ends communicate\nthrough the event without explicit locking.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nimport threading\nimport time\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Mapping, Optional\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME\",\n    \"DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_EXIT_CODE\",\n    \"DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_REASON\",\n    \"DISK_BUDGET_RETENTION_ADVICE\",\n    \"DEFAULT_DISK_BUDGET_MB\",\n    \"DEFAULT_DISK_POLL_TICK_SEC\",\n    \"DiskBudgetPoller\",\n]\n\n\n# Process exit code returned by the Phase 4 CLI dispatcher when the\n# poller has signalled a breach. Matches the design-spec §4 exit-code\n# table: ``2`` = harness error. The disk-budget breach is a harness-\n# enforced abort, not a per-eval failure, so it uses the same code as\n# the loader / scratch-root / reporter-contract violations.\nDISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_EXIT_CODE: int = 2\n\n# Filename the poller writes when a breach is detected. Pinned here so\n# the orchestrator, Reporter, and integration tests all reference one\n# constant. ``.json`` because the payload is a structured\n# :class:`BreachDetail` projection.\nDISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME: str = \"disk_budget_exceeded.json\"\n\n# ``skip_reason`` token written into every synthesised SKIPPED outcome\n# the orchestrator emits for unsubmitted specs after a breach. Stable\n# value so the Reporter / CLI consumers can match on it.\nDISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_REASON: str = \"disk_budget_exceeded\"\n\n# Design-spec §4 default budget. The CLI flag default mirrors this so a\n# bare ``superclaude eval run`` is bounded out of the box.\nDEFAULT_DISK_BUDGET_MB: int = 1024\n\n# Design-spec §13 polling cadence. Five seconds is a coarse-enough tick\n# that the recursive ``stat()`` walk does not become a measurable share\n# of runtime even for a 15-eval suite emitting transcripts at a few MB/s.\nDEFAULT_DISK_POLL_TICK_SEC: float = 5.0\n\n\n_BYTES_PER_MB: int = 1024 * 1024\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass BreachDetail:\n    \"\"\"Side-car payload describing a disk-budget breach.\n\n    Serialised verbatim into :data:`DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME`\n    by the poller. The Reporter surfaces this dict back through\n    ``RunSummary.artifacts['disk_budget_exceeded']`` so reviewers can\n    diagnose \"why did the run exit 2\" without re-running the poller.\n\n    Attributes\n    ----------\n    reason:\n        Always :data:`DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_REASON`. Pinned so consumers\n        can match on the constant token without parsing structure.\n    output_dir:\n        Absolute path of the directory the poller was watching, as a\n        string (paths do not JSON-serialise without a converter).\n    usage_bytes:\n        Measured cumulative size of the directory tree at the breach\n        tick. ``int`` so the value round-trips through JSON losslessly.\n    budget_bytes:\n        Effective budget in bytes (``max_disk_mb * 1024 * 1024``).\n    max_disk_mb:\n        Original budget the operator configured, in megabytes. Stored\n        alongside ``budget_bytes`` so the side-car is readable without a\n        mental conversion.\n    ticked_at:\n        ISO-8601 timestamp of the breach tick. Stamped via\n        :func:`time.gmtime` + :func:`time.strftime` so the value is\n        timezone-stable (UTC ``Z``).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    reason: str\n    output_dir: str\n    usage_bytes: int\n    budget_bytes: int\n    max_disk_mb: int\n    ticked_at: str\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict:\n        \"\"\"Return a JSON-serialisable dict in field-declared order.\"\"\"\n        return {\n            \"reason\": self.reason,\n            \"output_dir\": self.output_dir,\n            \"usage_bytes\": self.usage_bytes,\n            \"budget_bytes\": self.budget_bytes,\n            \"max_disk_mb\": self.max_disk_mb,\n            \"ticked_at\": self.ticked_at,\n        }\n\n\nclass DiskBudgetPoller:\n    \"\"\"Background-thread poller enforcing the NFR-PERF4 disk budget.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    output_dir:\n        Directory whose recursive size is summed every tick. Created if\n        missing only when ``ensure_output_dir=True``; otherwise the\n        first tick on a non-existent directory measures ``0`` bytes.\n    max_disk_mb:\n        Budget in megabytes. ``0`` disables the poller entirely (see\n        module docstring). Negative values raise :class:`ValueError`.\n    tick_sec:\n        Seconds between size measurements. Defaults to\n        :data:`DEFAULT_DISK_POLL_TICK_SEC` (5 s). Tests pass a small\n        value (eg. 0.05) to keep the suite fast.\n    artifact_name:\n        Filename of the breach side-car. Defaults to\n        :data:`DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME`. Tests substitute a\n        unique name when several pollers share a directory.\n    clock:\n        Optional ``() -> str`` returning the ``ticked_at`` ISO-8601\n        timestamp. Tests inject a deterministic clock; the default\n        formatter uses :func:`time.gmtime` so production output is\n        timezone-stable.\n\n    Notes\n    -----\n    The recursive size walk uses :meth:`pathlib.Path.rglob` and\n    :meth:`pathlib.Path.stat` with ``follow_symlinks=False`` so the\n    poller cannot be tricked into double-counting via a symlink into a\n    sibling tree. Files that disappear between the directory walk and\n    the ``stat()`` call (eg. an in-flight runner cleaning up its scratch\n    HOME) are silently ignored — a transient OS error must not crash the\n    poller thread.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    DEFAULT_TICK_SEC = DEFAULT_DISK_POLL_TICK_SEC\n    DEFAULT_MAX_DISK_MB = DEFAULT_DISK_BUDGET_MB\n    ARTIFACT_NAME = DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME\n    BREACH_REASON = DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_REASON\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        output_dir: Path,\n        *,\n        max_disk_mb: int = DEFAULT_DISK_BUDGET_MB,\n        tick_sec: float = DEFAULT_DISK_POLL_TICK_SEC,\n        artifact_name: str = DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME,\n        ensure_output_dir: bool = False,\n        clock: Optional[\"callable[[], str]\"] = None,\n    ) -> None:\n        if not isinstance(max_disk_mb, int) or isinstance(max_disk_mb, bool):\n            raise TypeError(\n                f\"max_disk_mb must be int; got {type(max_disk_mb).__name__}\"\n            )\n        if max_disk_mb < 0:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"max_disk_mb must be >= 0; got {max_disk_mb} \"\n                \"(use 0 to disable the budget)\"\n            )\n        if tick_sec <= 0:\n            raise ValueError(f\"tick_sec must be > 0; got {tick_sec}\")\n        if not artifact_name:\n            raise ValueError(\"artifact_name must be non-empty\")\n\n        self._output_dir = Path(output_dir)\n        if ensure_output_dir:\n            self._output_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n\n        self._max_disk_mb = int(max_disk_mb)\n        self._budget_bytes = int(max_disk_mb) * _BYTES_PER_MB\n        self._tick_sec = float(tick_sec)\n        self._artifact_name = artifact_name\n        self._clock = clock or _default_iso_clock\n\n        self._stop_event = threading.Event()\n        self._breach_event = threading.Event()\n        self._detail: Optional[BreachDetail] = None\n        self._artifact_path: Optional[Path] = None\n        self._thread: Optional[threading.Thread] = None\n        self._lifecycle_lock = threading.Lock()\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Read-only inspection surface\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    @property\n    def enabled(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"``True`` when the poller will actually spawn a thread.\"\"\"\n        return self._max_disk_mb > 0\n\n    @property\n    def max_disk_mb(self) -> int:\n        return self._max_disk_mb\n\n    @property\n    def budget_bytes(self) -> int:\n        return self._budget_bytes\n\n    @property\n    def tick_sec(self) -> float:\n        return self._tick_sec\n\n    @property\n    def output_dir(self) -> Path:\n        return self._output_dir\n\n    @property\n    def artifact_name(self) -> str:\n        return self._artifact_name\n\n    def is_breached(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"``True`` once a breach has been recorded.\n\n        One-shot — once the flag flips it never resets for the lifetime\n        of the poller. The orchestrator polls this between submissions\n        to decide whether to keep scheduling.\n        \"\"\"\n        return self._breach_event.is_set()\n\n    def breach_detail(self) -> Optional[Mapping[str, object]]:\n        \"\"\"Return the breach payload as a plain dict, or ``None``.\n\n        The mapping mirrors :class:`BreachDetail.to_dict`. Returns\n        ``None`` until :meth:`is_breached` reports ``True``.\n        \"\"\"\n        detail = self._detail\n        if detail is None:\n            return None\n        return detail.to_dict()\n\n    def artifact_path(self) -> Optional[Path]:\n        \"\"\"Path of the side-car file the poller wrote, or ``None``.\n\n        Equal to ``output_dir / artifact_name`` once a breach has been\n        recorded; ``None`` while the poller is healthy.\n        \"\"\"\n        return self._artifact_path\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Lifecycle\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def start(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Spawn the background polling thread.\n\n        No-op when :attr:`enabled` is ``False`` (``max_disk_mb=0``) or\n        when the thread is already running. Idempotent so the\n        orchestrator can call :meth:`start` unconditionally without\n        guarding the disabled case.\n        \"\"\"\n        with self._lifecycle_lock:\n            if not self.enabled:\n                return\n            if self._thread is not None and self._thread.is_alive():\n                return\n            self._stop_event.clear()\n            self._thread = threading.Thread(\n                target=self._poll_loop,\n                name=f\"cliEval-disk-budget-{self._max_disk_mb}MB\",\n                daemon=True,\n            )\n            self._thread.start()\n\n    def stop(self, *, timeout: Optional[float] = None) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Signal the background thread to exit and join it.\n\n        ``timeout`` is forwarded to :meth:`threading.Thread.join`. The\n        method is idempotent and safe to call from any thread; signal-\n        handler installers MAY call it from the main thread without\n        knowing whether the poller was ever started.\n        \"\"\"\n        with self._lifecycle_lock:\n            thread = self._thread\n            self._stop_event.set()\n        if thread is not None:\n            thread.join(timeout=timeout)\n\n    def __enter__(self) -> \"DiskBudgetPoller\":\n        self.start()\n        return self\n\n    def __exit__(self, exc_type, exc, tb) -> None:\n        self.stop()\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Polling internals\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _poll_loop(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Tick-driven measurement loop.\n\n        Uses :meth:`threading.Event.wait` rather than :func:`time.sleep`\n        so :meth:`stop` can shortcut the next tick immediately — the\n        orchestrator does not want to wait up to ``tick_sec`` seconds\n        for the poller to notice it is being shut down.\n        \"\"\"\n        while not self._stop_event.wait(self._tick_sec):\n            usage_bytes = self._compute_usage()\n            if usage_bytes > self._budget_bytes:\n                self._record_breach(usage_bytes)\n                return\n\n    def _compute_usage(self) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Sum every regular file under ``output_dir`` non-recursively-of-symlinks.\n\n        Returns ``0`` when the directory does not exist yet; lets the\n        poller run before the orchestrator has created its run\n        directory without raising. Per-file ``OSError`` is swallowed so\n        a vanishing in-flight scratch file does not crash the poller.\n        \"\"\"\n        root = self._output_dir\n        if not root.is_dir():\n            return 0\n        total = 0\n        # ``rglob('*')`` returns every entry including directories and\n        # symlinks. We only count regular files (``is_file()`` returns\n        # ``False`` for symlinks to files thanks to ``follow_symlinks``\n        # behaviour of the underlying ``stat`` — but we also guard with\n        # ``is_symlink()`` to be explicit about the policy).\n        for entry in root.rglob(\"*\"):\n            if entry.is_symlink():\n                continue\n            try:\n                if not entry.is_file():\n                    continue\n                total += entry.stat().st_size\n            except (OSError, FileNotFoundError):\n                # Transient: file was reaped between ``rglob`` and\n                # ``stat``. Skip — the next tick will catch any\n                # persistent growth.\n                continue\n        return total\n\n    def _record_breach(self, usage_bytes: int) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Persist the side-car and flip the breach flag.\n\n        The detail is built *before* the side-car is written so a\n        partial-write failure (eg. directory was deleted out from under\n        us) still leaves a valid in-memory payload for the Reporter.\n        The breach flag is set *after* the detail is published so any\n        consumer that reads :meth:`breach_detail` after observing\n        :meth:`is_breached` ``True`` is guaranteed a non-``None``\n        return.\n        \"\"\"\n        detail = BreachDetail(\n            reason=self.BREACH_REASON,\n            output_dir=str(self._output_dir),\n            usage_bytes=int(usage_bytes),\n            budget_bytes=self._budget_bytes,\n            max_disk_mb=self._max_disk_mb,\n            ticked_at=self._clock(),\n        )\n        artifact_path = self._output_dir / self._artifact_name\n        try:\n            self._output_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n            artifact_path.write_text(\n                json.dumps(detail.to_dict(), indent=2, sort_keys=True) + \"\\n\",\n                encoding=\"utf-8\",\n            )\n        except OSError:\n            # Filesystem unavailable — record the breach in memory only\n            # so the orchestrator still observes the flag. The side-car\n            # absence is a known degraded mode documented in the spec.\n            artifact_path = None\n\n        # Publish detail before flipping the flag (release-then-set\n        # ordering — see module docstring).\n        self._detail = detail\n        self._artifact_path = artifact_path\n        self._breach_event.set()\n\n\ndef _default_iso_clock() -> str:\n    \"\"\"Return the current UTC time as an ISO-8601 ``Z`` string.\n\n    Centralised so :class:`DiskBudgetPoller` and the side-car payload\n    use a single timestamp format. ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n#\n# Design-spec §11 (NFR-PERF2) pins peak resident-set-size at 2.25 GB for\n# ``--parallel 15``. When an operator requests the maximum concurrency on a\n# host that cannot afford that ceiling, the doctor MUST emit a warning\n# string containing the literal ``\"2.25 GB\"`` token so the operator can\n# tell at a glance which budget was insufficient. Hosts with less than\n# 4 GB free RAM are out-of-scope per ``CLAUDE.md`` infrastructure notes —\n# the warning is the documented stop-gap, not an exit-2 hard failure\n# (which would block legitimate ``--parallel 8`` runs on tight hosts).\n\nRAM_CEILING_GB: float = 2.25\n\"\"\"Design-spec §11 NFR-PERF2 peak-RSS ceiling at ``--parallel 15``.\n\nThe literal value ``2.25`` (not the bytes) is the one operators see in\nthe warning text, so changing it requires a roadmap edit. The bytes\nconstant below is derived from it so the two views cannot drift.\n\"\"\"\n\nRAM_CEILING_BYTES: int = int(RAM_CEILING_GB * (1024**3))\n\"\"\"Byte-equivalent of :data:`RAM_CEILING_GB` for free-RAM comparisons.\n\n``int(2.25 * 1024**3) == 2_415_919_104``. Computed once at import time so\nthe helper does not re-derive it on every call.\n\"\"\"\n\nRAM_CEILING_TEXT: str = f\"{RAM_CEILING_GB} GB\"\n\"\"\"Canonical rendering of the ceiling used in operator-facing strings.\n\nThe T03.17 AC asserts that the warning contains this literal token. Pin\nit here so any downstream string formatting picks up changes uniformly.\n\"\"\"\n\nPARALLEL_RAM_GATE_THRESHOLD: int = 15\n\"\"\"``--parallel`` value at-or-above which the RAM precheck fires.\n\nMatches :attr:`RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL`. Below this threshold the\nbenchmark does not document a ceiling, so the doctor stays silent rather\nthan emit a misleading number.\n\"\"\"\n\n\ndef _default_free_ram_probe() -> Optional[int]:\n    \"\"\"Return free RAM in bytes via Linux ``/proc/meminfo``.\n\n    Prefers ``MemAvailable`` (kernel ≥3.14) because it accounts for\n    reclaimable cache the way an oncoming allocation would; falls back to\n    ``MemFree`` on ancient kernels. Returns ``None`` on any failure (eg\n    non-Linux host, locked-down container) so the doctor records a\n    ``probe unavailable`` SOFT-SKIP rather than crashing — a doctor that\n    crashes on tight hosts is worse than one that warns.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    meminfo = Path(\"/proc/meminfo\")\n    if not meminfo.is_file():\n        return None\n    try:\n        text = meminfo.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    except OSError:\n        return None\n    available: Optional[int] = None\n    free: Optional[int] = None\n    for line in text.splitlines():\n        key, _, rest = line.partition(\":\")\n        key = key.strip()\n        if key not in (\"MemAvailable\", \"MemFree\"):\n            continue\n        tokens = rest.strip().split()\n        if not tokens:\n            continue\n        try:\n            kb = int(tokens[0])\n        except ValueError:\n            continue\n        unit = tokens[1].lower() if len(tokens) > 1 else \"kb\"\n        # /proc/meminfo always reports in kB on Linux; the unit check is\n        # defensive in case a future kernel changes the format.\n        multiplier = 1024 if unit == \"kb\" else 1\n        value = kb * multiplier\n        if key == \"MemAvailable\":\n            available = value\n        elif key == \"MemFree\":\n            free = value\n    if available is not None:\n        return available\n    return free\n\n\ndef _check_free_ram_for_parallel(\n    *,\n    requested_parallel: Optional[int],\n    probe: Optional[Callable[[], Optional[int]]] = None,\n    ceiling_bytes: int = when the probe returned ``None`` — the host\n      cannot self-report free RAM so we cannot certify the budget either.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if requested_parallel is None or requested_parallel < threshold_parallel:\n        return None\n\n    probe = probe or _default_free_ram_probe\n    description = (\n        f\"free RAM >= {RAM_CEILING_TEXT} for --parallel {threshold_parallel}\"\n    )\n    try:\n        free_ram = probe()\n    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — probe is caller-supplied\n        return CapabilityStatus(\n            name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n            passed=False,\n            failure_mode=\"skip\",\n            description=description,\n            detail=(\n                f\"free-RAM probe raised {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}; \"\n                f\"{RAM_CEILING_TEXT} budget unverified\"\n            ),\n        )\n    if free_ram is None:\n        return CapabilityStatus(\n            name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n            passed=False,\n            failure_mode=\"skip\",\n            description=description,\n            detail=(\n                f\"free-RAM probe unavailable on this host; cannot verify \"\n {free_gb:.2f} GB < {RAM_CEILING_TEXT} ceiling \"\n                f\"for --parallel {threshold_parallel}\"\n            ),\n        )\n    return CapabilityStatus(\n        name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n        passed=True,\n        failure_mode=\"skip\",\n        description=description,\n        detail=f\"free RAM {free_gb:.2f} GB >= {RAM_CEILING_TEXT}\",\n    )\n\n\ndef _check_ptytest_vendored(pty_dir: Optional[Path] = None) -> CapabilityStatus:\n    \"\"\"SOFT-SKIP check: ptytest vendored under ``cli/eval/pty/``.\n\n    The actual ptytest vendoring Extends :meth:`CapabilityReport.to_json` with a ``coverage_gate``\n    marker so callers can detect whether ``--check-coverage`` was passed.\n    The base ``CapabilityReport`` keys are preserved The loader is injectable\n    for tests that want to stub schema/regex/capability gates without\n    touching the file system.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    loader = loader if loader is not None else SuiteLoader()\n    summaries: list[SuiteSummary] = []\n    for manifest in discover_suite_manifests(suites_dir):\n        parsed: ParsedSuite = loader.load(manifest)\n        summaries.append(\n            SuiteSummary(\n                name=parsed.name,\n                version=parsed.version,\n                eval_count=len(parsed.evals),\n                source=manifest,\n            )\n        )\n    return summaries\n\n\ndef render_list_text(summaries: Iterable[SuiteSummary]) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Render the human-readable ``eval list`` output.\"\"\"\n\n    rows = list(summaries)\n    lines = [\"superclaude eval list:\"]\n    if not rows:\n        lines.append(\"  (no suites found)\")\n        return \"\\n\".join(lines)\n    for row in rows:\n        lines.append(\n            f\"  - check_coverage: bool,\n    output_dir: Optional[Path],\n    parallel: Optional[int],\n    suite: Optional[str],\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Verify host preconditions for ``superclaude eval run``.\n\n    Exit codes:\n\n    * ``0`` — every HARD capability satisfied.\n    * ``2`` — at least one HARD capability failed, or ``--output-dir``\n      was supplied and escaped the OPS-002 / AC12 scratch-root              click.echo(f\"eval doctor: {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}\", err=True)\n                sys.exit(SUITE_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE)\n            except SuiteLoaderError as exc:\n                click.echo(\n                    f\"eval doctor: {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}\", err=True\n                )\n                sys.exit(SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE)\n        coverage_result = coverage_gate(\n            settings_path=Path.home() / \".claude\" / \"settings.json\",\n            suite=suite_specs,\n            output_dir=None,\n        )\n\n    if as_json:\n        payload = click.echo(_format_coverage_summary(coverage_result))\n\n    # NFR-PERF2 SOFT-SKIP warning: when ``--parallel >= 15`` was requested\n    # and the host could not certify the free-RAM budget, emit a CLI callers\n    MUST map this to :data:`EVAL_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        eval_id: str,\n        suite_name: str,\n        known_ids: Iterable[str],\n    ) -> None:\n        self.eval_id = eval_id\n        self.suite_name = suite_name\n        self.known_ids: tuple[str, ...] = tuple(known_ids)\n        known = \", \".join(self.known_ids) if self.known_ids else \"<none>\"\n        super().__init__(\n            f\"eval {eval_id!r} not in suite {suite_name!r} (known: {known})\"\n        )\n\n\ndef _evalspec_to_dict(spec: EvalSpec) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Project an ``EvalSpec`` into a manifest-shaped dict.\n\n    Field ordering matches ``suite.schema.json`` so YAML/JSON output is\n    deterministic and round-trips visually against the source manifest.\n    Optional fields are omitted when they hold their dataclass default\n    (e.g. ``category=\"\"``, empty tuples, ``timeout_sec=None``) so the\n    \"no editorial transformation\" guarantee from T01.22 Notes holds: an\n    eval that did not declare ``category:`` in YAML does not gain one\n    here.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    payload: dict[str, Any] = {\n        \"id\": spec.id,\n        \"title\": spec.title,\n    }\n    if spec.category:\n        payload[\"category\"] = spec.category\n    if spec.requires:\n        payload[\"requires\"] = list(spec.requires)\n    if spec.timeout_sec is not None:\n        payload[\"timeout_sec\"] = spec.timeout_sec\n    if spec.isolation is not None:\n        payload[\"isolation\"] = dict(spec.isolation)\n    if spec.inputs:\n        payload[\"inputs\"] = [dict(row) for row in spec.inputs]\n    if spec.expects:\n        payload[\"expects\"] = [dict(row) for row in spec.expects]\n    if spec.parameterize:\n        payload[\"parameterize\"] = [dict(row) for row in spec.parameterize]\n    if spec.no_pty is not None:\n        payload[\"no_pty\"] = spec.no_pty\n    return payload\n\n\ndef _parsed_suite_to_dict(parsed: ParsedSuite) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Project a :class:`ParsedSuite` into the manifest envelope shape.\n\n    ``evals`` is the post-parameterize-expansion list (the canonical\n    \"what would run if you invoked ``eval run``\" view per FR-CLI3 AC).\n    Suite-level fields are verbatim from the manifest so the output\n    mirrors the validated source byte-for-byte modulo the ``evals``\n    expansion.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    return {\n        \"name\": parsed.name,\n        \"version\": parsed.version,\n        \"description\": parsed.description,\n        \"defaults\": dict(parsed.defaults),\n        \"required_binaries\": [dict(row) for row in parsed.required_binaries],\n        \"optional_capabilities\": [dict(row) for row in parsed.optional_capabilities],\n        \"evals\": [_evalspec_to_dict(spec) for spec in parsed.evals],\n    }\n\n\ndef resolve_suite_manifest(suite: str, suites_dir: Path) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Resolve ``--suite <token>`` to a manifest path on disk.\n\n    Resolution precedence (first match wins):\n\n    1. ``token`` is itself a file path that exists → return it.\n    2. ``suites_dir / f\"{token}.yaml\"`` exists → return it (filename stem\n       convention used by the built-in suites).\n    3. Otherwise scan ``suites_dir`` and return the first manifest whose\n       schema-validated ``name`` field equals ``token``. Broken manifests\n       in the same directory are skipped silently — we only fail on the\n       *requested* suite, not on the neighbours.\n\n    Raises:\n        SuiteNotFound: No manifest matched the token under any rule. CLI\n            callers map this to :data:`SUITE_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    candidate = Path(suite)\n    if candidate.is_file():\n        return candidate\n\n    by_filename = suites_dir / f\"{suite}.yaml\"\n    if by_filename.is_file():\n        return by_filename\n\n    loader = SuiteLoader()\n    for manifest_path in discover_suite_manifests(suites_dir):\n        try:\n            parsed = loader.load(manifest_path)\n        except SuiteLoaderError:\n            # Neighbour manifest is broken; don't let it mask the lookup.\n            continue\n        if parsed.name == suite:\n            return manifest_path\n\n    raise SuiteNotFound(suite, suites_dir)\n\n\ndef describe_suite(\n    suite: str,\n    *,\n    suites_dir: Path,\n    eval_id: Optional[str] = None,\n    loader: Optional[SuiteLoader] = None,\n) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Resolve, load, and project a suite (optionally a single eval).\n\n    Validation runs via :class:`SuiteLoader` BEFORE any rendering, so\n    schema / id-regex / capability rejections raise before the caller\n    can produce stdout (FR-CLI3 AC: validate before print).\n\n    Args:\n        suite: Suite name, filename stem, or direct manifest path. See\n            :func:`resolve_suite_manifest` for resolution precedence.\n        suites_dir: Directory to search when ``suite`` is not a direct\n            path. Tests inject a tmp dir via ``--suites-dir``.\n        eval_id: Optional post-expansion eval id (``E1``, ``E2.1``) to\n            filter the output to a single eval. Missing → ``EvalNotFound``.\n        loader: Injectable :class:`SuiteLoader` for tests that need to\n            stub capability resolution.\n\n    Returns:\n        A dict suitable for ``yaml.safe_dump`` / ``json.dumps``. When\n        ``eval_id`` is ``None``, the dict is the full manifest envelope;\n        when ``eval_id`` is set, the dict is a single ``evalEntry`` shape\n        (the same projection :func:`_evalspec_to_dict` produces).\n\n    Raises:\n        SuiteNotFound: Resolution rule chain produced no manifest.\n        EvalNotFound: ``eval_id`` did not match any post-expansion id.\n        SchemaError / InvalidEvalId / UnresolvedCapability: Propagated\n            from :class:`SuiteLoader` so the CLI exit-code map still\n            applies (all → 2).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    manifest_path = resolve_suite_manifest(suite, suites_dir)\n    active_loader = loader if loader is not None else SuiteLoader()\n    parsed: ParsedSuite = active_loader.load(manifest_path)\n\n    if eval_id is None:\n        return _parsed_suite_to_dict(parsed)\n\n    for spec in parsed.evals:\n        if spec.id == eval_id:\n            return _evalspec_to_dict(spec)\n    raise EvalNotFound(\n        eval_id=eval_id,\n        suite_name=parsed.name,\n        known_ids=sorted(spec.id for spec in parsed.evals),\n    )\n\n\ndef render_describe_yaml(payload: dict) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Render a describe payload as a deterministic YAML document.\n\n    ``sort_keys=False`` preserves the manifest field ordering produced by\n    :func:`_parsed_suite_to_dict` / :func:`_evalspec_to_dict` so the\n    YAML    default_flow_style=False,\n        allow_unicode=True,\n    )\n\n\ndef render_describe_json(payload: dict) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Render a describe payload as a deterministic JSON document.\n\n sort_keys=True)\n\n\n@eval_group.command(\"describe\")\n@click.option(\n    \"--suite\",\n    \"suite\",\n    required=True,\n    help=(\n        \"Suite name, filename stem, or path to a manifest. Returns ``None`` on any failure (eg\n    non-Linux host, locked-down container) so the doctor records a\n    ``probe unavailable`` SOFT-SKIP rather than crashing — a doctor that\n    crashes on tight hosts is worse than one that warns.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    meminfo = Path(\"/proc/meminfo\")\n    if not meminfo.is_file():\n        return None\n    try:\n        text = meminfo.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    except OSError:\n        return None\n    available: Optional[int] = None\n    free: Optional[int] = None\n    for line in text.splitlines():\n        key, _, rest = line.partition(\":\")\n        key = key.strip()\n        if key not in (\"MemAvailable\", \"MemFree\"):\n            continue\n        tokens = rest.strip().split()\n        if not tokens:\n            continue\n        try:\n            kb = int(tokens[0])\n        except ValueError:\n            continue\n        unit = tokens[1].lower() if len(tokens) > 1 else \"kb\"\n        # /proc/meminfo always reports in kB on Linux; the unit check is\n        # defensive in case a future kernel changes the format.\n        multiplier = 1024 if unit == \"kb\" else 1\n        value = kb * multiplier\n        if key == \"MemAvailable\":\n            available = value\n        elif key == \"MemFree\":\n            free = value\n    if available is not None:\n        return available\n    return free\n\n\ndef _check_free_ram_for_parallel(\n    *,\n    requested_parallel: Optional[int],\n    probe: Optional[Callable[[], Optional[int]]] = None,\n    ceiling_bytes: int = RAM_CEILING_BYTES,\n    threshold_parallel: int = PARALLEL_RAM_GATE_THRESHOLD,\n) -> Optional[CapabilityStatus]:\n    \"\"\"SOFT-SKIP check: free RAM is sufficient for the requested concurrency.\n\n    Returns ``None`` (gate inactive) when ``requested_parallel`` is below\n    :data:`PARALLEL_RAM_GATE_THRESHOLD` — the design-spec only pins the\n    RSS ceiling for the upper-bound run, so warning operators about\n    insufficient RAM at ``--parallel 8`` would be noise.\n\n    When the gate is active the helper returns a :class:`CapabilityStatus`\n    whose ``failure_mode`` is ``\"skip\"`` regardless of outcome:\n\n    * Passed when the probe returns a value at or above\n      :data:`RAM_CEILING_BYTES`.\n    * Failed (SOFT-SKIP) when free RAM is below the ceiling, with the\n      detail string containing the literal :data:`RAM_CEILING_TEXT` token\n      so a grep against the operator-facing artifact pins the failure\n      mode without parsing structure.\n    * Failed (SOFT-SKIP) when the probe returned ``None`` — the host\n      cannot self-report free RAM so we cannot certify the budget either.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if requested_parallel is None or requested_parallel < threshold_parallel:\n        return None\n\n    probe = probe or _default_free_ram_probe\n    description = (\n        f\"free RAM >= {RAM_CEILING_TEXT} for --parallel {threshold_parallel}\"\n    )\n    try:\n        free_ram = probe()\n    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — probe is caller-supplied\n        return CapabilityStatus(\n            name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n            passed=False,\n            failure_mode=\"skip\",\n            description=description,\n            detail=(\n                f\"free-RAM probe raised {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}; \"\n                f\"{RAM_CEILING_TEXT} budget unverified\"\n            ),\n        )\n    if free_ram is None:\n        return CapabilityStatus(\n            name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n            passed=False,\n            failure_mode=\"skip\",\n            description=description,\n            detail=(\n                f\"free-RAM probe unavailable on this host; cannot verify \"\n                f\"{RAM_CEILING_TEXT} budget\"\n            ),\n        )\n    free_gb = free_ram / (1024**3)\n    if free_ram < ceiling_bytes:\n        return CapabilityStatus(\n            name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n            passed=False,\n            failure_mode=\"skip\",\n            description=description,\n            detail=(\n                f\"free RAM {free_gb:.2f} GB < {RAM_CEILING_TEXT} ceiling \"\n                f\"for --parallel {threshold_parallel}\"\n            ),\n        )\n    return CapabilityStatus(\n        name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n        passed=True,\n        failure_mode=\"skip\",\n        description=description,\n        detail=f\"free RAM {free_gb:.2f} GB >= {RAM_CEILING_TEXT}\",\n    )\n\n\ndef _check_ptytest_vendored(pty_dir: Optional[Path] = None) -> CapabilityStatus:\n    \"\"\"SOFT-SKIP check: ptytest vendored under ``cli/eval/pty/``.\n\n    The actual ptytest vendoring lands at M2; the row stays SOFT-SKIP so\n    doctor still exits 0 on a clean dev machine until the upstream task\n    materialises ``cli/eval/pty/__init__.py``.\n    \"\"\"\n    target = pty_dir if pty_dir is not None else Path(__file__).resolve().parent / \"pty\"\n    init_file = target / \"__init__.py\"\n    passed = init_file.is_file()\n    return CapabilityStatus(\n        name=\"vendored.ptytest\",\n        passed=passed,\n        failure_mode=\"skip\",\n        description=\"ptytest vendored under cli/eval/pty/\",\n        detail=str(target) if passed else f\"{init_file} not found (vendored at M2)\",\n    )\n\n\ndef _extend_report(\n    base: CapabilityReport,\n    extras: tuple[CapabilityStatus, ...],\n) -> CapabilityReport:\n    \"\"\"Return a new ``CapabilityReport`` with ``extras`` appended and\n    failure-bucket tallies updated.\n\n    The base report's ``blocked_evals`` and ``skip_flags`` are preserved\n    verbatim — supplementary doctor rows only contribute to the three\n    failure buckets (``hard_failures``, ``soft_skips``, ``soft_xfails``).\n    \"\"\"\n    rows = base.report + extras\n    hard_failures = list(base.hard_failures)\n    soft_skips = list(base.soft_skips)\n    soft_xfails = list(base.soft_xfails)\n    for status in extras:\n        if status.passed:\n            continue\n        if status.failure_mode == \"hard\":\n            hard_failures.append(status.name)\n        elif status.failure_mode == \"skip\":\n            soft_skips.append(status.name)\n        else:  # \"xfail\"\n            soft_xfails.append(status.name)\n    return replace(\n        base,\n        report=rows,\n        hard_failures=tuple(hard_failures),\n        soft_skips=tuple(soft_skips),\n        soft_xfails=tuple(soft_xfails),\n    )\n\n\ndef build_doctor_report(\n    skip_flags: Optional[Iterable[str]] = None,\n    *,\n    gates: Optional[CapabilityGates] = None,\n    claude_version_probe: Optional[Callable[[], Optional[str]]] = None,\n    claude_home: Optional[Path] = None,\n    pty_dir: Optional[Path] = None,\n    config: Optional[EvalConfig] = None,\n    requested_parallel: Optional[int] = None,\n    free_ram_probe: Optional[Callable[[], Optional[int]]] = None,\n) -> CapabilityReport:\n    \"\"\"Aggregate :class:`CapabilityGates` + doctor-specific checks.\n\n    Args:\n tuple[str, str]:\n    \"\"\"Return ``(glyph, tag)`` for the human checklist line.\"\"\"\n    if row.passed:\n        return \"[ok]\", \"\"\n    if row.skipped_by_flag:\n        return \"[--]\", \" \" (xfail)\"\n\n\ndef render_checklist(report: CapabilityReport) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Render the doctor green-checklist text (FR-CLI4 human output).\"\"\"\n    lines = [\"superclaude eval doctor:\"]\n AC.\"\"\"\n    by_name = {row.name: row for row in report.report}\n    lines = [\"eval doctor: HARD failures\"]\n    for name in report.hard_failures:\n        row = by_name.get(name)\n        if row doctor_payload(\n    report: CapabilityReport,\n    *,\n    check_coverage: bool,\n    coverage_result: Optional[CoverageResult] = None,\n) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Build the ``--json`` payload.\n\n CLI callers\n    MUST map this to :data:`EVAL_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        eval_id: str,\n        suite_name: str,\n        known_ids: _evalspec_to_dict(spec: EvalSpec) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Project an ``EvalSpec`` into a manifest-shaped dict.\n\n    Field ordering matches ``suite.schema.json`` so YAML/JSON output is\n    deterministic and round-trips visually against the source manifest.\n    Optional fields are omitted when they hold their dataclass default\n    (e.g. ``category=\"\"``, empty tuples, ``timeout_sec=None``) so the\n    \"no editorial transformation\" guarantee from T01.22 Notes holds: an\n    eval that did not declare ``category:`` in YAML does not gain one\n    here.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    payload: dict[str, Any] = ``--suite <token>`` to a manifest path on disk.\n\n    Resolution precedence (first match wins):\n\n    1. ``token`` is itself a file path that exists → return it.\n    2. ``suites_dir / f\"{token}.yaml\"`` exists → return it (filename stem\n       convention used by the built-in suites).\n    3. Otherwise scan ``suites_dir`` and return the first manifest whose\n       schema-validated ``name`` field equals ``token``. Broken manifests\n       in the same directory are skipped silently — we only fail on the\n       *requested* suite, not on the neighbours.\n\n    Raises:\n        SuiteNotFound: No manifest matched the token under any rule. CLI\n            callers map this to :data:`SUITE_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    candidate = Path(suite)\n    if candidate.is_file():\n        return candidate\n\n    by_filename = suites_dir / f\"{suite}.yaml\"\n    if by_filename.is_file():\n        return by_filename\n\n    loader = SuiteLoader()\n    for manifest_path in discover_suite_manifests(suites_dir):\n        try:\n            parsed = loader.load(manifest_path)\n        except SuiteLoaderError:\n            # Neighbour manifest is broken; don't let it mask the lookup.\n            continue\n        if parsed.name == suite:\n            return manifest_path\n\n    raise SuiteNotFound(suite, suites_dir)\n\n\ndef describe_suite(\n    suite: str,\n    *,\n    suites_dir: Path,\n    eval_id: Optional[str] = None,\n    loader: Optional[SuiteLoader] = None,\n) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Resolve, load, and project a suite (optionally a single eval).\n\n    Validation runs via :class:`SuiteLoader` BEFORE any rendering, so\n    schema / id-regex / capability rejections raise before the caller\n    can produce stdout (FR-CLI3 AC: validate before print).\n\n    Args:\n        suite: Suite name, filename stem, or direct manifest path. Missing → ``EvalNotFound``.\n        loader: Injectable :class:`SuiteLoader` for tests that need to\n            stub capability resolution.\n\n    Returns:\n        A dict suitable for ``yaml.safe_dump`` / ``json.dumps``. When\n        ``eval_id`` is ``None``, the dict is the full manifest envelope;\n        when ``eval_id`` is set, the dict is a single ``evalEntry`` shape\n        (the same projection :func:`_evalspec_to_dict` produces).\n\n    Raises:\n        SuiteNotFound: Resolution rule chain produced no manifest.\n        EvalNotFound: ``eval_id`` did not match any post-expansion id.\n        SchemaError / InvalidEvalId / UnresolvedCapability: Propagated\n            from :class:`SuiteLoader` so the CLI exit-code map still\n            applies (all → 2).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    manifest_path = {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_018KN7Ewr1PqkdcaW1xrQBfK","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01KzTxYcM5TRvVbRmvHxBfY5","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/docs/eval/retention.md","content":"# Eval Artifact Retention Policy (OPS-003)\n\n**Status:** Stable as of T04.21 (Phase 4, D-0081 / R-081). Pins the\noperator-visible retention contract for `superclaude eval run` artifacts\non every termination path (clean exit, per-eval FAIL/ERROR, harness\nabort on disk-budget breach, SIGINT/SIGTERM cancellation).\n\n## TL;DR\n\n`superclaude eval run` is **biased toward preserving evidence**. The\ndefault behaviour is:\n\n* The **run directory** under `--output-dir` (default `.dev/eval-runs/<ISO>/<run-id>/`)\n  is **never cleaned up automatically**, regardless of exit code.\n* **Run-level summaries** (`summary.md`, `summary.json`, `junit.xml`)\n  are written before the process exits and are retained on every\n  termination path — including SIGINT, harness abort, and disk-budget\n  breach.\n* **Per-eval HOME directories** under the scratch root follow status:\n  * PASS → removed (default) unless `--keep-home` is passed.\n  * FAIL / ERRORED / TIMEOUT → **always kept** for forensic inspection.\n  * Setup failure (NFR-ISO2) → kept with a `.eval-meta/setup_failed`\n    tag pinning the exception class / message.\n* On a **disk-budget breach** (exit code 2) the dispatcher writes a\n  `disk_budget_exceeded.json` side-car and emits an\n  [OPS-003 retention-advice string](#disk-budget-breach-advice) to\n  stderr so the operator knows what was preserved and which knobs to\n  turn on the next invocation.\n\n```text\nDiskBudgetPoller.ARTIFACT_NAME = \"disk_budget_exceeded.json\"\nDISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_EXIT_CODE  = 2\nHomeIsolation.SETUP_FAILED_TAG_RELPATH = \".eval-meta/setup_failed\"\nEvalRunner._finalize keep semantics: keep = True if status != \"PASS\" else keep_home_on_pass\n```\n\n## 1. Run directory: never auto-cleaned\n\n`--output-dir` (default the FR-G4 layout root `.dev/eval-runs/<ISO>/<run-id>/`)\nis treated as **append-only** by the harness. Nothing the orchestrator\nwrites is ever rewound. Specifically:\n\n* The orchestrator never deletes a previously-written run directory.\n* The Reporter writes `summary.md`, `summary.json`, and (when\n  `--junit-xml` is on) `junit.xml` *just before* the process exits on\n  every termination branch documented in [design-spec §4][ds-4] —\n  including:\n  * Clean run (exit 0)\n  * One or more FAIL / ERROR / TIMEOUT outcomes (exit 1)\n  * Harness contract violation / disk-budget breach (exit 2)\n  * Cooperative cancellation (SIGINT/SIGTERM → exit 3)\n* The summaries record `counts.expanded == K` against\n  `counts.manifest_n == N'` so the FR-RPT1 invariant is preserved even\n  on a partial run.\n\nOperators are expected to garbage-collect old run directories manually\n(or via a periodic CI cleaner). The harness does not ship its own\nsweeper because there is no safe default — a CI runner that times out\nits job at the 15-minute mark must NOT discover its artifacts have been\nswept by a sibling job's harness invocation.\n\n[ds-4]: ../../.dev/releases/current/cliEval/design-spec.md\n\n## 2. `--keep-home`: per-eval HOME retention on PASS\n\n`--keep-home` is a Click flag forwarded into\n`EvalRunner.keep_home_on_pass`. The default is **False** — PASS evals\nhave their scratch HOME (`<scratch-root>/<run-id>/<eval-id>/`) removed\nduring the FR-LC1 step-7 teardown. Failing evals, errored evals, and\ntimed-out evals **always** preserve their HOME for inspection\nregardless of `--keep-home`.\n\n| Outcome status | `--keep-home` False (default) | `--keep-home` True |\n|---|---|---|\n| PASS | HOME removed | HOME preserved |\n| FAIL | HOME preserved | HOME preserved |\n| ERRORED | HOME preserved | HOME preserved |\n| TIMEOUT | HOME preserved | HOME preserved |\n| SKIPPED | (HOME never allocated) | (HOME never allocated) |\n| Setup failure (NFR-ISO2) | HOME preserved, tagged | HOME preserved, tagged |\n\nThe keep/remove decision is centralised in\n`src/superclaude/cli/eval/runner.py::_finalize`:\n\n```python\nkeep = True if status != \"PASS\" else keep_home_on_pass\n_safe_teardown(home, keep=keep, on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error)\n```\n\nTeardown errors are routed through `on_teardown_error` so they cannot\nflip a PASS into a FAIL — the design-spec invariant is that teardown\nis best-effort.\n\n## 3. NFR-ISO2: setup failures always preserve the HOME\n\nThe atomic-setup contract (NFR-ISO2 / T02.13) catches any exception\nthrown by the per-eval setup pipeline (`HomeIsolation.acquire`),\nwrites a `.eval-meta/setup_failed` artifact tag inside the partially-\nmaterialised HOME, and re-raises the exception unchanged. The HOME is\n**not** torn down on the failure path; the runner reports the eval as\nERRORED and the HOME remains on disk with its setup_failed tag.\n\nThe tag's filename is pinned by\n`HomeIsolation.SETUP_FAILED_TAG_RELPATH = \".eval-meta/setup_failed\"`.\nThe first line of the tag is the exception class (e.g.\n`builtins.PermissionError`), followed by the stringified message and\nthe traceback. Operators can grep for this file under the scratch root\nto find every HOME that crashed during setup.\n\nThis policy applies regardless of `--keep-home`: setup failures\nalways preserve evidence because the failure happened *before* any\nper-eval body could write a transcript that would otherwise carry the\ndiagnostic signal.\n\n## 4. Disk-budget breach: advice + side-car\n\nOn every disk-budget breach (NFR-PERF4 — see\n[`docs/eval/runtime.md`](runtime.md) and design-spec §13), the\n`DiskBudgetPoller` writes a `disk_budget_exceeded.json` side-car into\nthe run directory naming the measured usage, budget, output dir, and\nbreach timestamp. The orchestrator synthesises `SKIPPED` outcomes for\nevery unsubmitted spec with `skip_reason=\"disk_budget_exceeded\"` so the\nN'-vs-K invariant is preserved. In-flight workers run to completion;\nthe poller never kills a worker.\n\n### Disk-budget breach advice\n\nBefore exiting with code 2 the Phase 4 dispatcher emits this verbatim\nstring to stderr:\n\n```text\ndisk-budget exceeded: the run was aborted to protect the host filesystem. The run directory, summary.{md,json}, junit.xml, per-eval artifacts for finished evals, and disk_budget_exceeded.json side-car are retained for forensic inspection. Raise --max-disk-mb (or set to 0 to disable the budget) and/or pass --keep-home False on the next invocation; see docs/eval/retention.md for the full OPS-003 retention policy.\n```\n\nThe advice text is pinned in\n`src/superclaude/cli/eval/disk_budget.py::DISK_BUDGET_RETENTION_ADVICE`\nand is the **single source of truth** for the OPS-003 retention\nadvice. This document quotes that constant verbatim, the CLI dispatcher\nechoes that constant verbatim, and\n`tests/cli/eval/test_retention_policy.py` asserts both surfaces carry\nthe same bytes. Drift between the doc, the CLI, and the constant is\ntherefore a test failure rather than a silent operator surprise.\n\n## 5. Cancellation (SIGINT/SIGTERM)\n\nWhen the cooperative cancellation token fires (exit code 3 per\n[design-spec §4][ds-4]) the orchestrator stops accepting new submissions\nbut lets in-flight workers complete naturally. Every outcome the\nrunner emitted is committed to `summary.{md,json}` / `junit.xml`\nbefore the process exits. The run directory is not cleaned up;\noperators can re-run with `--eval <id>` to retry the specs that were\ncancelled mid-flight (see [retry.md](retry.md)).\n\n## 6. Summary retention matrix\n\n| Exit code | Run dir | summary.{md,json} | junit.xml | Per-eval artifacts (passed evals) | Per-eval artifacts (failed evals) | Per-eval HOMEs |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| 0 (clean) | kept | kept | kept (when `--junit-xml`) | kept | n/a | passes follow `--keep-home`; others n/a |\n| 1 (failures) | kept | kept | kept | kept | kept | passes follow `--keep-home`; failures kept |\n| 2 (harness abort) | kept | kept (partial run, N'-vs-K preserved) | kept | kept | kept | passes follow `--keep-home`; failures + setup-fail kept; unsubmitted = SKIPPED (no HOME) |\n| 3 (interrupted) | kept | kept (partial run) | kept | kept | kept | passes follow `--keep-home`; in-flight cancelled outcomes kept |\n\n## 7. Operator playbook\n\nWhen a `superclaude eval run` exits with code 2 (disk-budget breach),\nthe operator can recover with this sequence:\n\n1. Read the OPS-003 stderr advice (rendered immediately before the\n   exit) for the run-dir path and the recommended knobs.\n2. `cat <run-dir>/disk_budget_exceeded.json` to confirm which budget\n   was breached (`usage_bytes`, `budget_bytes`, `ticked_at`).\n3. Inspect `<run-dir>/summary.md` / `summary.json` for the partial\n   run — `counts.expanded` will be < `counts.manifest_n` and\n   `counts.skipped` will include the `disk_budget_exceeded` skip\n   reason.\n4. Garbage-collect old run dirs under `--output-dir` (the harness will\n   never auto-prune them) and re-run with a larger `--max-disk-mb`\n   (or `--max-disk-mb 0` to disable the budget when CI gives you a\n   dedicated runner).\n5. To re-run only the unfinished specs, pass\n   `--eval <id1> --eval <id2> ...` for the SKIPPED rows. See\n   [retry.md](retry.md) for the subset-replay contract.\n\nWhen a `superclaude eval run` exits with code 1 (per-eval failures):\n\n1. Inspect `<run-dir>/summary.md` for the FAIL / ERROR / TIMEOUT\n   rows. Each row's `artifacts` mapping cross-links to\n   `per-eval/<eval-id>/logs.jsonl` and `per-eval/<eval-id>/tty.transcript`.\n2. The per-eval HOME under `<scratch-root>/<run-id>/<eval-id>/` is\n   preserved — `cd` into it to reproduce the failing eval's\n   filesystem state.\n3. `ls **/.eval-meta/setup_failed` under the scratch root to find any\n   setup failures and pivot to their captured exception.\n\n## See also\n\n* [`docs/eval/runtime.md`](runtime.md) — disk-budget poller (NFR-PERF4),\n  concurrency knobs, and timeout semantics.\n* [`docs/eval/scratch-roots.md`](scratch-roots.md) — scratch-root\n  resolution rules and the `.eval-meta` directory layout.\n* [`docs/eval/retry.md`](retry.md) — re-running failed evals via\n  `--eval <id>`.\n* [`.dev/releases/current/cliEval/design-spec.md`][ds-4] — §4 CLI\n  surface, §10 risk matrix (R4), §13 NFR-PERF4 disk-budget poller.\n* [`.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0081/spec.md`](../../.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0081/spec.md)\n  — D-0081 deliverable spec for OPS-003.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":127,"cache_read_input_tokens":100892,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":127,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":52,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"dfc568c9-b6ce-4a44-9f46-8a06ea2e751a","uuid":"17d8e1a2-6d51-4491-921f-42b2498b83d2"}
